W385 Write path substitution. A real Agent run authorized an external overwrite and an injected parser redirected it to a missing workspace path. Before the fix the workspace marker was created even though no entry receipt was issued. The existing runtime observer fixture also assumed external overwrite was disabled; the ordinary refusal controls now explicitly use overwrite:false. Successful writes are independently exercised in the compiled PTY.

W385 release probe. On this Darwin temporary volume, after an open original was renamed aside and a substitute installed under its former name, renameatx_np with RENAME_SECLUDE | RENAME_EXCL returned ENOTSUP (45). The installed kernel library exports unlink and unlinkat, with no funlinkat entry. This probe establishes no compare-and-delete primitive. Continue #35 while disposal remains open. The older summary-only compaction entry was already superseded in CURRENT.

W385 disposition boundary. An unprivileged native host probe outside the Codex sandbox returned EPERM for F_SETLEASE on a temporary directory and ENOPOLICY (103) for the vnguard ping. The local SDK exposes F_SETLEASE but availability of the constant does not establish authority. The archive now records capture intent and completion separately on the session gate. Neither marker authorizes unlink of a reused name, truncation of a relocated file, or success inferred from an absent file. [Apple lease implementation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/vfs/vfs_subr.c).

W385 mixed archive. An occupied second slot leaves the first verified hold archived. A retry after clearing the fixture conflict verifies both locations and completes. The initial PTY expected a full error message in the one-line status area, which truncates it. The driver now checks the visible text and both inodes. The sandbox prevented ps-based stopped-child checks in the rewind suite. The host rerun passed all 71 tests. The terminal receipt may retain a pending state when the writer died after journal commitment but before publishing the commit marker. The committed archive verifies the original branch rows against the active journal in this case. Older live rollbacks with no surviving successor journal cannot establish that proof.

W385 archive review. Moving the enclosing directory retains the parent inode in the held-file receipt. A late swap can place a different directory in the archive; the post-move verifier and retry refuse that slot, preserving its contents. The autocomplete regression suite also exposed two previously absent help entries and a wraparound expectation for the former last checkpoint row. The catalog and fixture were updated.

W385 native stage cleanup. The old failure path closed its stage FD then unlinked a reused name. The old CLI retention fixture also wrapped the now-registered built-in Write and was refused by the runtime. Removing the wrapper revealed the fixture allowed only Write. Its retained-overwrite case now allows and executes full Read on UTF-8 data, then Write; binary checkpoint fixtures continue to cover arbitrary bytes.

W385 pending handoff review. An initial verification checked the departing grant after runtime teardown. releaseResources deliberately invalidates it. Check the departing and copied leases before release and the copied incarnation after binding. The changed-transaction PTY initially matched one more character than the status bar displayed. It now checks the visible prefix, source UUID, changed transaction bytes and original filesystem state.

2026-11-07 post-commit inheritance. The live rewind appends references after clearing the transaction. A process exit in that interval leaves the archived transaction and a complete branch without references. Reuse the authorized inheritance append only on the exact branch. Any retained torn journal goes through the existing torn-record verifier.

2026-11-07 successor gate review. Session.openInternal holds flock during lock publication and closes that descriptor on return. Reuse acquireBranchGate during committed recovery and validate the active owner's token, named gate and exact journal under flock. The active-source control preserved the transaction.

2026-11-07 post-rename recovery. The first PTY checks timed out because the status bar clipped the diagnostic after “needs read”. Match the displayed prefix there and assert the full mode-specific diagnostic in the agent matrix. Both corrected PTY checks passed.

2026-11-07 normalization review. The first passing matrix exposed two missing negative controls on inspection. An older captured object has no normalization receipt digest, and the final held/landed report runs only on the success tail. Require a bound digest when saved capture evidence accompanies an error and reject an error after the final identity report. Existing non-error evidence remains readable. New negative controls remove the digest, substitute another valid error and forge the contradictory final records. Both matrices and compiled CLI chains were rerun on the final build. Classification falls under existing S-2/S-4/S-5. All executed runs passed.

2026-11-07 ordinary pre-effect error matrix. The first run passed 11/12 scenarios. The reverse replacement pre-mkdir negative control emitted the expected phase-mismatch error, then its 15-second child timeout returned null status. This scenario took 48.2 seconds. pmset recorded idle sleep at 09:50:12 for 48 seconds and maintenance sleep at 09:51:09 for 49 seconds during the run. Both CLI chains and the early preparation driver passed. Repeat the same matrix at the identical build under a process-lifetime idle-sleep assertion. This is a host timing issue under S-1/S-5.

2026-11-07 captured-error continuation. The first type check caught an attempt ID access on the prepared stage, which has no ID. Narrow the stage to an attempted variant before comparing the persisted continuation ID and digest. This preserves the evidence union and the pre-effect boundary under S-2/S-4.

2026-11-07 partial creation error review: source/native inspection showed terminal error and exit are separate writes, each interruptible, while the inspector recognized only a complete pair. Keep all possible error families for exact partial grammar instead of guessing one from an incomplete name; filter by preparation/publication and pre/post-mode protocol order, then require the existing physical proof. Actual EIO followed by SIGKILL during terminal writes covers both directions and opaque staging. Negative/overflow/leading-zero errno, fake exit zero, extra lines and conflicting suffixes refuse with transaction bytes unchanged. New and existing tests pass first attempt. Classification: incomplete protocol coverage; existing S-2/S-4/S-6, no guard weakened.

2026-11-07 torn creation mode-report review: native create_retained changes mode and syncs before mode_applied, while receipt_append can leave a strict byte prefix on death. The inspector required a complete frame or no report bytes, retaining independently provable pre-publication attempts. Match the received prefix to the original staging's current verified frame, preserving the raw bytes and snapshot rather than filling in evidence. Readable desired-byte checks and opaque metadata restrictions remain unchanged. Actual deaths, conflicting suffix controls and both CLI directions pass first attempt. Classification: incomplete protocol coverage, existing S-2/S-4/S-6; terminal-error prefixes remain separate.

2026-11-07 unreadable staging investigation: owned mode-0000 probes showed both O_RDONLY and O_EVTONLY fail EACCES, including outside the sandbox. An ACL-readable original source works only in the escalated fixture; new staging remains unreadable. Use named metadata under checked parent FDs, explicitly content-unverified, never permission mutation or byte adoption. Native metadata/link/bracket drivers and the first cold death case passed. The EIO negative-control fixture correctly rejected a fake exit-zero receipt but its existing regex omitted the new metadata-only error; expanded only the error assertion. One combined patch used an incorrect question-file heading and was rejected atomically; reapplied with observed context. Classification: platform assumption, fixture diagnostic and patch context; no product guard weakened. The first compiled CLI fixture then tried to overwrite its read-only baseline before immediately deleting it and failed EACCES before recovery. Skip that unnecessary fixture write for the zero-mode forward-create scenario, preserving the inaccessible mode and the existing unlink step; product behavior unchanged.

2026-11-07 create post-mode/error review: native create_retained sets mode_effect before fchmod and emits mode_applied only after subsequent sync/identity checks. The reader previously required the pre-chmod ctime or a complete mode_applied frame, and rejected every error trailer, retaining recoverable readable cases. Adapted it to source-backed phase order plus independently verified original inode/parent/full desired bytes/mode and a retained current snapshot; accept only complete matching error/exit-1 pairs while retaining their original bytes. Native test-only EIO cases and true deaths in both directions, wrong family/exit controls and inode replacement all pass first attempt. Inaccessible 0000 and partial-error receipts remain unresolved. Classification: incomplete protocol/state coverage, existing S-2/S-4/S-6; no guard or timeout weakened.

2026-11-07 creation persistence review: source inspection found forward and inverse callers saving attempting before the creation coordinator allocated/bound the preparation receipt. An exit in that gap leaves a correctly unresolved legacy-looking attempt despite no target call. Move creation’s first save into its existing coordinator so both receipt identities and phase are recorded together; leave replacement/removal ordering and cold readers unchanged. Four owned process-exit cases assert the first attempted record is bound and recover in both directions. Check/build, 29 affected cases and two compiled CLI chains pass first attempt. Classification: persistence ownership/order, existing S-4/S-6; older unbound records remain unknown.

2026-11-07 retained creation validation interruption: the final 84-case matrix passed 82 and reported one failed forward-linked/create subcase plus its parent. Its corruption reader emitted the expected invalid-descriptor refusal, but spawnSync returned status null at the 15-second process limit; the enclosing subcase took 529 seconds. Read the exact assertion and process invocation, confirmed the main handle terminal and no remaining owned fixture/test processes, then inspected pmset evidence: system sleep at 08:37:37, DarkWake at 08:45:20 and full wake at 08:45:29 overlap the run. Treat this as a sleep-interrupted exit observation, not a passing test or a reason to change product guards/timeouts. Re-run only W385_LINKED_ONLY at unchanged input/build identity. Classification: environment/process observation; final retry result recorded in the package.

2026-11-07 retained creation integration: initial affected matrix exited 1 with seven failed subcases plus their parent. Cold reader added a publication digest even to already-completed live records; committed archive byte equality failed, and repeated compensation cleanup changed previously frozen transaction bytes. Read checkpoints.ts receipt archive comparison and transaction persistence; fix the producer to independently verify both complete receipts and persist publication digest with the completed outcome, before archive creation. Keep cold promotion for genuinely interrupted attempts. The remaining initial cases, including both 20-cut legacy linked chains, passed. Added preparation-death, same-byte preparation-receipt replacement and archived-snapshot controls; final results recorded in the package. Classification: producer/consumer durability ordering, S-4/S-6; no weakened byte equality or inode check.

2026-11-07 linked integration review: the isolated binding fixture used one owner for source and successor, hiding an overstrict source.id === historical grant.owner check. Read encodeDirectoryRewindPlan, branch.source and existing cold verifier: historical owner is the successor while fresh authority belongs to the original checkpoint Session. Removed only that equality, retaining checkpoint-owner, current grant owner/dev/ino/fd/incarnation checks, and added actual forward/reverse handoff recovery. Also bound the normalization receipt digest on completed promotion so its archived bytes cannot change silently; tests substitute receipt inode, corrupt pair metadata and alter completed receipt bytes. These were integration/review corrections, not failed runtime tests; real crash/regrant/normalization/compensation scenarios now pass.

2026-11-07 normalization race fixtures: target-before initially expected the foreign target to remain at its original path, but the existing capture rename can move a replacement arriving after the final check into held before detecting mismatch. Verified foreign bytes survive in held, original pair survives separately, and receipt reports unknown. The parent-before fixture then renamed the grant root itself; retained FD authority correctly kept operating on that opened root. Moved the target into a nested subdirectory and replaced that named component instead, exercising same_parent rejection. Both are fixture-contract errors, not reasons to weaken inode or path guards. Final 22 scenarios pass and all owned fixtures were removed.

2026-11-07 linked binding source lookup: guessed directory-file-state.test.ts and directory-checkpoint.test.ts did not exist. Located the actual directory-checkpoint-bracket.test.ts with rg --files and reused its real Session/grant fixture setup. Classification: repository path discovery; no product/test failure. Use inventory before choosing an unfamiliar test path.

2026-11-07 linked-pair fixtures: first assertion used status field 11 for nlink, but status_line defines nlink at 10 and parent device at 11. Replaced anonymous field assertions with named destructuring plus exact field count and parent checks. The checkpoint-bracket run then failed its stopped-helper assertion because sandbox ps returns operation-not-permitted; source processState converts that failure to empty state. Confirmed ps denial, reran with narrow process-observation permission and passed. Removed failed owned fixture after driver cleanup. Categories: protocol fixture indexing; environmental process visibility. No product identity guard relaxed.

2026-11-07 atomic-publication evidence collection: first native executable hash used a guessed directory-write basename; build-native.mjs identifies native/directory-write/write, whose hash was then collected. A documentation patch also used a partial line as full context and failed without changing the file. Use the build output path and exact current context. Classification: evidence-path/context lookup; no product or test failure.

2026-11-07 pre-effect recovery CLI: recovery and byte/mode checks passed, but the smoke driver still required every forward stage to be completed. Read its archived-stage assertion and updated only the new forward-untouched mode to require refused, absent rollback, the unchanged target inode and an archived no-effect attempt. Reverse untouched mode still requires a completed new rollback plus its abandoned-attempt receipt. No product gate or existing completed-effect assertion was relaxed; rerun both actual CLI chains.

2026-11-07 captured continuation retry: forward/reverse tests failed their exact pending-byte check after intent persistence followed by native death before publication. Read saveTransaction (raw JSON.stringify), the continuation schema field order and the differing byte prefix (receipt versus continuation). The continuation loop rewrote an unchanged intent after schema parsing reordered keys. Reuse an already-durable, semantically identical directory record rather than saving a no-op again. Preserve the strict byte assertion and rerun the interrupted sequence; this is idempotent intent persistence, not a reason to relax recovery proof.

2026-11-07 captured recovery integration: a multi-hunk test patch failed atomically because the reader driver passed `cut` directly, while the drafted context expected `cut ?? ''`. Read the exact run-wrapper lines and reapplied the edit against them. No product or fixture behavior changed from the failed patch; no assertion or permission rule was relaxed.

2026-11-07 partial native reconciliation audit: initial persisted-reconciliation reading bound receipt bytes by SHA but reused the saved result without rechecking intent/result consistency. Before delivery, add a pure V2 prefix/result validation on both initial promotion and every persisted read, alongside digest equality and actual branch/grant/file verification. The comparison frame is memory-only and never writes a fabricated exit marker. Added corruption control changes the intent inode and updates its saved digest together; recovery must still refuse without modifying the fixture. This audit follow-up requires rebuilding and rerunning the affected cold/CLI paths.

2026-11-07 partial native reconciliation: checkpoint-bracket regression driver stopped before native execution because mkdtemp returned /var/folders while Session requires the canonical /private/var/folders workspace. Read the driver's workspace construction and Session error; canonicalize the newly owned temporary fixture with realpathSync, preserving the product identity rule. This is a stale-driver path fixture, not a native recovery failure. Rerun the same driver after the correction; remove only its identified owned failed fixture.

2026-11-07 native result receipts: generic native cancellation regression lost `error not_renamed` because replacing dprintf diagnostics with the cancellation-aware write_all suppressed the final error after SIGTERM. Restored original vdprintf diagnostics and write_all phase semantics for pipe-only mode; receipt mode remains separately synchronized and incomplete on cancellation. The first adapter patch also failed exact-context matching because ordinary publication conditionally supplies mode; re-read that call and preserved its conditional argument. Native receipt identity must be bound before invocation by future transaction integration; current adapter support is not unknown-effect recovery.

2026-11-07 cold settled rollback: the first matrix rejected branch proof. Initial inspection found a real ordering hazard: recovery deletes the uncommitted journal before final verification, so branch verification was separated from external-state verification and moved before owned deletion. The rerun still failed before cleanup. The owning branchProof function returned bytes only for committed branches, leaving the new exact-prefix comparator without bytes for pending branches. Return the already-read/verified bytes for both dispositions; no prefix admission change. The initial diagnosis was incomplete. A further crash after branch deletion but before transaction unlink remains a retained-evidence gap, not verified recovery. No permission or unknown-effect gate was relaxed.

2026-11-07 mixed compensation: remove inverse initially failed because native removal's held observation names the quarantine parent. Read write.c lines 229-246 and used the before-image parent for missing-path republication, retaining the native parent identity check. All inverse cases then passed. CLI first wait searched a long injected error clipped by the status bar; full session-resumed message, filesystem/receipt assertions and a subsequent dialogue prove recovery. Forward and reverse observations describe different locations and must not be interchanged.

2026-11-07 live mixed restore: native missing/present union exposed held as unknown; validate it with the existing strict observation schema instead of casting. Successful external application still carried historical unavailable metadata, so the terminal incorrectly said those files were not restored; derive the success message from the completed coordinator path and verify through actual CLI. Existing death tests failed because sandbox ps returned no stdout; only the affected owned-process tests were rerun with access and passed. CLI loopback listen was denied EPERM in sandbox, then passed with local-listener access. These environment failures did not justify product-contract changes.

2026-11-07 mixed evidence: TypeScript correctly refused adding bytes to a union field inside an assert.throws callback because a mutable property could change after outer narrowing. Captured the narrowed before-state in a const; final check passes. Integration review also separated checkpoint-source owner from successor directory authority, and added plan-digest revalidation before serialization. Pending/committed cold fixtures include an actual workspace rollback operation so merely leaving unrelated bytes untouched cannot satisfy the recovery test.

2026-11-07 retained restore mode: initial native zero-mode case published but reported unknown because a post-publication O_EVTONLY open still required read permission. A direct probe returned EACCES. Reused the already-open staged descriptor through F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, preserving repeated hashes and no-follow path/inode comparisons. Final native normal/fault/race suite and restore adapters pass. Source RVr likewise keeps the target descriptor across chmod. Native source/executable are outside scripts/identity.mjs inventory, so their exact hashes are recorded separately with this validation.

2026-11-07 rewind planner integration review: source RVr restores backup mode, while the existing retained Write publisher always preserves the current target mode. Reusing that publisher unchanged would silently miss mode-only rewinds. Planner now carries before/after mode and tests mode-only changes; the eventual restore transaction must bind an explicit target mode. No restore success is claimed. Final checks ran after all test edits; the initial pre-final-fixture check is not used as final evidence.

2026-11-07 mixed rewind acceptance: old opt-in probe established a checkpoint before the target was tracked, then expected deletion from a later missing-file record. That record does not establish checkpoint-time absence under the current host contract. Replaced preparation with default Write tracking followed by an explicitly witnessed checkpoint. Initial present-file scenario correctly hit the stale-read guard after fixture bytes changed; added actual Read before the later Write. Type checking exposed the checkpoint helper's unwitnessed return variant; narrowed with the external_witness property before asserting it. Both corrected scenarios now fail specifically at missing external preview operations, preserving earlier-checkpoint negative controls.

2026-11-07 default external Write integration: first affected run found 20 failures among 63 selected tests. Fixtures had used the unsupported-write error as a backup-only operation, and transaction adapters caught that error before injecting faults. Enabling the default path both changed original targets and bypassed adapter assertions. Migrated existing-target backup fixtures to explicit overwrite:false refusals; adapter tests now remove only the publication capability from their preparation call, retaining it for the transaction under test. Missing-target and normal compiled CLI expectations now require actual publication; swapped implementations still fail before effects. Original bytes, modes, tamper and unknown checks are retained. Final check/build and 65 tests pass; two existing probes remain skipped. See .audit/w385-default-write-validation.json.

2026-11-07 shared Write preparation: extraction review caught an added workspaceReal lookup in the result helper; restored the caller's original relative-path calculation before validation so no extra path lookup or changed fallback is introduced. dno's old broad excerpt included the following declaration; refined to exact [733986,734328). All final checks passed. External publication integration remains explicitly open.

W385 reactive compaction validation (2026-11-06): SDK MessageParam admits system roles beyond journal schema, so compaction record construction narrows at that boundary. W385 lifecycle durability (2026-11-06): real writer fsync failure at admitted left a complete line that reopened with zero journal/directory sync calls. W385 nested observer binding (2026-11-06): ordinary child rows could be appended without actual job provenance. W385 registry lifecycle (2026-11-06): releaseSession removed owner routing before hook cancellation, so actual cancelled completion was absent from journal. W385 hook call identity (2026-11-06, focused repair verified): the real Agent/command/async-registry test first observed no toolUseId for PermissionRequest and FileChanged. W385 hook fixture types (2026-11-06, resolved locally): compound event filters did not narrow SessionEvent, producing TS2339 for toolUseId, parentMessageId and backgroundTasks. W385 retirement generation boundary (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first ID-wide fence caused the existing paused-result-file and paused-inline-approval tests to fail when binding a new Session under a closed predecessor's ID. The source suite then had 165 passes, two failures and one existing skip. Those assertions and their ordering remain unchanged. Retirement now retains each old scope and Session separately. An additional two-generation background control verifies that old cleanup does not remove the new owner and later release includes both captured generations. W385 repeated owner release (2026-11-06, initial ID-wide attempt, superseded by the generation repair above): real Session/background tests reproduce an early second acknowledgement and a missing expected rejection after failed cleanup. `releaseSession` previously deleted owner resources before awaiting them, so a later call saw an empty owner. Runtime now retains one cleanup promise per retiring owner, shares its actual result, preserves collected failures or thrown errors, blocks new scope/bind admission and includes retiring owners in root cleanup. Successful settlement removes the retirement record and allows a fresh scope. Two new regression groups, 113 related tests and four CLI/handoff tests pass. Installed and child validation remains pending. Category: removing an owner record lost the in-flight or failed settlement; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 compiled owner-release fixture (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first CLI fixture threw when it observed early acknowledgement, then tried to read a marker that had not been written. W385 post-commit start reference (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real Agent fixture changed the returned start object's ID and incarnation after its append returned. W385 concurrent package rebuild (2026-11-06, resolved by sequential validation): the package driver invokes `npm run build` and deletes `dist`. W385 actual-Write event schema (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first execution observation schema supplied two `runtime_write_execution` objects to the outer `kind` discriminated union, so Zod rejected duplicate discriminators during module initialization. One outer kind now checks the phase/outcome relationship in the boundary refinement; the product type retains its discriminated phase union and the filter narrows explicitly. Category: nested variants and narrowing did not fit the existing boundary API; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 evidence paragraph patch (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a manual patch supplied only the first phrase of a whole paragraph and did not match the file. Reapplying against the complete previously read line preserves the existing paragraph. Category: patch context must match a complete file line, as in the earlier port-line incident; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 runtime-owner package fixture (2026-11-06, resolved locally): `npm run smoke:package` first stopped at JavaScript parsing with `SyntaxError: Identifier 'runtimeModule' has already been declared`; the new installed-owner fixture reused a name already declared later in the package driver. After renaming the local fixture variable, `node --check` and the full offline package pass; the installed owner row and two cold readers succeed without upstream requests. Category: a new fixture declaration collided with an existing same-scope declaration; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 public Write-start issuer (2026-11-06, resolved within call-issuance scope): a caller used public `PathApprovalCallOwner`, an actual bound runtime wrapper and an assistant message to call the first implementation's exported `issueRuntimeWriteStart`, then appended an owner-bearing start without executing an Agent call. The focused test observed the forged row (`true !== false`). Issuance and single-use validation now live in the Agent module behind a private function; the public surface only reports a live owner for a wrapper. The same counterfeit, a forged tool context and the serialization/accessor cases are rejected, while actual Agent and compiled CLI controls pass. Category: a public issuer accepted caller-assembled evidence instead of owning the actual Agent call; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 port-line edit (2026-11-06, resolved locally): `apply_patch` could not match a small phrase inside the very long `Runtime-owned Write start result` line, although the bytes and phrase were confirmed unique. A scripted exact one-occurrence replacement updated only the regression count and was verified with `rg`. The documentation line itself remained intact. Category: the patch engine did not match a short span within an oversized single line; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 direct runtime Write control (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a direct wrapper call after an actual Agent start initially made no second backup because the fixture omitted the Session's working directories. Supplying them made the direct call reach the blocked branch, but the tool uses the same tool-use ID and validates the issuing assistant/user turn; a repeat after the Agent effect is refused rather than creating a duplicate. The retained control now checks one actual directory backup, one start row and the unchanged external file. Category: a direct-tool fixture omitted required host context, and the initial expectation did not account for the existing backup-message identity; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 isolated child regression (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the combined skill/isolation suite reported 93 pass, one skip and two failures, both `Child session owner mismatch` in standalone isolated child cases. The shared fixture binds a real parent Session and builds its scoped tools but invoked them with a `ToolContext` that omitted that Session. The existing runtime `childOwner` checks the actual context Session; this code and fixture mismatch predate the runtime-Write change. Passing the bound parent restores the actual child paths; the focused pair and full skill/isolation suite pass without weakening the owner check. Category: a direct-tool fixture omitted the Session supplied by the Agent; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 runtime-owned Write serialization reds (2026-11-06, open): a `tool_start.name` getter added a forged owner after the initial check and the event was accepted. An inherited enumerable `toJSON` was rejected by the strict schema, but making the inherited method non-enumerable made a valid title event serialize as a forged owner-bearing `tool_start`; both cases were accepted in the focused red. Category: validating live properties before serialization did not bind the actual journal bytes to the authorized event; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 runtime-owned Write start accessor red (2026-11-06, open): a caller supplied a `tool_start` whose `runtimeWriteOwner` getter returned `undefined` to the first authorization check and a forged owner afterward. The first implementation read the getter, skipped authorization and accepted the event; the test failed with `Missing expected exception` before its no-getter-effect assertion. Category: a security-sensitive optional property was checked by value instead of inspecting an inert own data descriptor; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 runtime-owned Write start red (2026-11-06, open): the existing real two-Session runtime fixture was extended to require a Session-bound runtime owner observation on B's own Write start while leaving A's borrowed wrapper unmarked. B's own `tool_start` has no such observation, so the assertion failed with `undefined` instead of B's ID; the later direct-forgery assertion was not reached. Category: a journaled tool start does not identify its selected runtime wrapper and owner generation; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 nonempty source-lock positive probe (2026-11-06, open): at identity `8da5f21d21a088de1882a849442c2a78f7769614577b2a5eb93f05644023a749`, `W385_NONEMPTY_SNAPSHOT_PROBE=1` reached the actual compiled CLI normal nonempty-snapshot case and failed its expected `/Checkpoint recovery source lock reclaimed/` assertion because that notice was absent. The assertion stopped this opt-in run before the subsequent lock-file assertion; the normal recovery regression had separately verified lock retention at the preceding test identity with the same product inputs and build. Category: durable tool/observer/hook effect provenance remains insufficient to admit the nonempty suffix; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 runtime wrapper owner provenance (2026-11-06, open): a real runtime bound same-workspace Sessions A and B, then B's Agent used A's registered builtin Write wrapper while A's lease was live. The blocked call made a verified backup owned by B, just as B's own wrapper did; after A's release the borrowed wrapper rejected a later call. Two cold readers retained B's backup owner and no live grant. This owned fixture does not establish how production CLI chooses its tools. Category: live wrapper scope is not evidence that its owner and the executing Session coincide; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 post-result observer provenance (2026-11-06, open): after completed recovery, a real Agent executed the unchanged registered builtin Write wrapper. Its caller-supplied `toolResult` callback confirmed the matching `tool_result` was the last event and created a workspace file after `Session.append` returned from its fsync. The result text and event-kind sequence match the ordinary no-observer case. The actual resumed CLI retained the witnessed source lock, and two independent cold processes verified the nonempty snapshot without a live grant. This does not claim that the CLI's own observer writes files. Category: the effect interval extends beyond tool execution, hooks and result persistence to caller-owned observer callbacks; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 compiled outer-dispatch fixture (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first CLI attempt intercepted the first registered wrapper and changed it, but the actual later call returned the ordinary blocked-Write error without the injected effect. CLI first prepares an initial tool set and then binds the Session's own tool set; capturing the first registration did not establish the actual Agent's selected object. The fixture now obtains the selected object from the real Agent at its run entry; replaced `execute` and injected `executeBlocks` are rejected, and the normal CLI Write controls pass. Category: an initialization object was mistaken for the live dispatch object; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 builtin Write outer dispatch (2026-11-06, resolved within main-Agent dispatch scope): a real Agent used a registered builtin Write runtime wrapper whose outer `execute` was replaced, changed to an effectful getter, or whose `executeBlocks` was injected as a function or an effectful getter. All four created a workspace file and returned the ordinary blocked-Write error without a directory backup. The unchanged wrapper made a real backup and returned the same error; a separately cloned custom tool executed its own `executeBlocks`. The main Agent now invokes the registered outer executor only after checking its own method, absent block method and prototype; real Agent, compiled CLI, installed and cold controls pass. This does not authenticate callbacks, hooks or other tool consumers. Category: checking the inner builtin executor does not bind the Agent's outer dispatch target; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 related native-removal regression (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the combined tools/runtime/path-prototype run reported 108 pass and one failure in `bound directory removal checks the current target and retains uncertain effects`. A scenario-labelled rerun identified `wrong-bytes`: the native helper captures the original inode into the private `held` name before comparing supplied bytes, then retains it with `unknown` on mismatch. This matches the existing retained-capture record but the fixture still expected the public name to exist. The fixture now checks both public-name absence and original bytes under `held`; the isolated case and all 109 related regressions pass. No native behavior changed. This path does not call the builtin Write runtime wrapper. Category: a neighboring regression retained an outdated post-capture expectation; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 compiled Write fixture type check (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the new compiled CLI and actual Agent substitution tests passed, but `tsc --noEmit` reported TS7022 on the compiled fixture's `spawnSync` result. An explicit `SpawnSyncReturns<string>` annotation restored type checking without changing runtime behavior. Category: overloaded process-result inference failed in the new fixture; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 CLI fixture lookup (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a search named a nonexistent smoke-driver path, so `rg` exited 2 while also finding relevant CLI options. Subsequent fixture work uses the existing lifecycle driver. Category: an assumed driver filename was not verified before searching; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 builtin Write substitution (2026-11-06, resolved within executor scope): a real Agent invoked a `bindRuntimeTool`-wrapped default Write after its fixture refresh callback replaced `execute` with a same-error function. The first test failed because the Agent received the replacement's blocked-Write text instead of an implementation-change error; the marker assertion was initially after the failing assertion and was not reached. Moving that assertion first reproduced the actual extra workspace file (`true !== false`). The wrapper now checks the registered builtin executor after its callbacks and invokes that pinned method; altered functions and accessors are rejected. Real Agent, compiled CLI, normal controls, installed package and cold readers pass. Wrapper callback and hook effects remain independently unproven. Category: mutable host refresh could change the implementation between wrapper binding and execution; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 caller-controlled runtime wrapper (2026-11-06, open): an actual Agent used the default blocked Write through `bindRuntimeTool` with a fixture `refresh` callback that created a workspace file and then left the original Write implementation intact. Its event kinds and blocked error matched the ordinary no-hook case, while the subsequent CLI retained the witnessed source lock and two cold readers verified the snapshot. Existing read-identity tests also show a caller-controlled refresh can substitute the underlying implementation. This fixture does not establish that production `refreshPluginComponents` performs either effect. Category: recognizing the inner tool alone does not authenticate the runtime wrapper or its callbacks; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 asynchronous blocked-Write failure hook (2026-11-06, open): actual compiled CLI regranted a directory, invoked the default Write through its runtime, and started an async `PostToolUseFailure` command. A fixture barrier released the hook only after the `tool_result` append returned; the hook then created a workspace file while the next local provider response waited. The CLI subsequently captured the nonempty snapshot and retained the old source lock. The real hook is configured in the fixture Session metadata, the provider makes no network requests, and two cold readers retain the backup without reviving a grant. Category: an async hook may still have effects after a complete result row, so result persistence alone is not hook settlement; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 blocked Write failure-hook provenance (2026-11-06, open): a real Agent used the default blocked external Write and a real `PostToolUseFailure` command hook created a workspace file after `tool_effect` and before `tool_result`. The fixture installed this hook on the live Session configuration; two cold readers observed the backup but no live grant and no hook in the cold configuration. The resumed CLI retained the source lock. This is a local live-config fixture, not an actual settings UI. The existing tool-input authorization and pre-write backup do not record the actual implementation or establish the absence of later hook effects. Category: effects and live execution provenance cannot be inferred from a result or later configuration; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 completed recovery followed by nonempty snapshot (2026-11-06, open): after a complete recovery fact, a real Agent regranted the directory, attempted a currently blocked external Write, and completed a second text turn. Its history contains a verified existing-file backup and a nonempty snapshot, while actual `--resume --recover-lock` leaves the matching dead source lock. The suffix includes `tool_start,directory_backup,tool_effect,tool_result` before the later snapshot; the default blocked tool left the owned fixture file unchanged. A separate instrumented tool wrapper wrote another workspace file and rethrew the same error; both scenarios had the same event kinds and effect-result text, retained the source lock, and exposed available snapshot backups to two cold readers without restoring grants. The positive-reclaim probe remains red. Evidence sufficient to distinguish actual tool implementation and hook/effect outcomes is still needed. Category: bounded cleanup excludes a later nonempty snapshot and its preceding tool/history records; error text alone cannot establish the absence of other effects; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 completed recovery followed by empty snapshot (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): after a fully recorded torn-recovery fact, a real local Agent appended `message,directory_snapshot_intent,directory_snapshot,message,turn_end`. Its empty directory snapshot and receipt validated, but actual CLI resume left the matching dead source lock. The host now locates the already completed fact across complete empty-snapshot text turns, validates all current receipts and rechecks the fact under the existing store and Session gates before reclaiming the exact dead source lock. Repeated snapshots, corruption of the earlier receipt, missing/pending receipts, cleanup-process death, installed CLI and cold readers pass. The same snapshot attempt while the original torn inheritance is unresolved remains fenced; nonempty snapshots remain open. Category: the bounded completed-fact cleanup excluded a later independently receipted snapshot; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 later ordinary conversation (2026-11-06, resolved within plain-text scope): a real local Agent with a scripted provider appended `message,message,turn_end` after blocked CLI recovery; both the torn branch and a completed recovery fact then remained ineligible. The proof now accepts only complete human text, matching text-only assistant response and normal `end_turn` triples, with full-prefix validation and current directory-history verification before source-lock reclaim. A later negative expectation incorrectly treated `directoryHistoryDisabled` as unexplained: the real Agent wrote it while checkpoints were disabled, and recovered `SI` [4731065,4731207) and `Tke` [4732048,4733481) confirm the per-turn gate. That local feature-toggle fixture is not an actual settings UI. Real CLI with a no-network fixture, incomplete/tool/snapshot, mismatch/tamper and process-death controls, installed successor and cold readers pass. Category: bounded suffix admission excluded completed ordinary turns; an initial test expectation overlooked the recorded per-turn gate; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 later target file-history events (2026-11-06, resolved within workspace-path scope): a real local `write_file` call appended its `file_history` row after a blocked CLI resume, and the next resume did not reconcile or reclaim a witnessed dead source lock. The first adaptation admitted every schema-valid row, but real CLI controls showed it also crossed a no-path shell event whose actual shell created an untracked external file. Admission now requires a normalized workspace-relative path; missing or outside paths and permission changes remain ineligible. Tracked/untracked local writes, changed-path proof, writer deaths, installed successor and cold readers pass. Category: suffix admission first excluded workspace history, then failed to distinguish unknown or outside history; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 later target title events (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): two actual CLI tests resumed under a live checkpoint store lock, issued `/rename retained-title` and `/color blue`, then resumed after the lock was released. Torn inheritance and completed recovery previously remained ineligible. Strictly validated title rows can now join color rows in the bound later prefix; changed titles invalidate proof, and a later file-history event prevents automatic recovery or source-lock reclamation. Actual CLI, process-death, installed successor and cold-reader controls pass. Category: the bounded restart proof treated a valid display-title row as a checkpoint-state change; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 later target color events (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): two actual CLI tests resumed while a live checkpoint store lock prevented recovery, issued `/color blue`, and then resumed after the lock was released. A torn inheritance and a completed recovery fact previously stopped reconciling or reclaiming because the last row was the color event. Startup now recognizes only valid trailing color rows, binds the entire current prefix and separately matches the original branch prefix against retained torn bytes. Real tampering, unsupported-title, further writer-death, installed successor and cold-reader controls pass. Other later event types remain open. Category: restart recovery selected only the last journal event, discarding an earlier candidate after a harmless later event; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 restart reconciliation/store lock (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): an independent live process held the checkpoint store lock before any transaction file existed, yet the actual CLI reported torn inheritance reconciliation. Startup now takes the same lock as compliant transaction writers before checking the file, revalidating the evidence and applying either recovery effect. Actual CLI, independent restore contender, process-death and post-effect cleanup-failure controls, installed package and cold readers pass. Category: file-existence admission was not synchronized with transaction publication; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 completed-recovery source lock (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): a real CLI recovery writer was killed after writing the complete recovery fact, and target resume left the dead source Session lock. New facts now bind non-secret source lock identity at the authorized append; `--recover-lock` reclaims only the matching dead owner after full proof validation. Live, changed, legacy and unconfirmed replacement locks remain. Actual CLI, process-death cuts, installed package and independent cold readers pass. Category: a completed cross-Session recovery lacked a persisted source-lock identity for cleanup; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 post-recovery-created checkpoint torn inheritance (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): a recovered Session regrants its directory, executes an Agent turn and captures a fresh checkpoint. Its next fork partially wrote the inheritance row and returned `unknown` because the candidate included a `post-torn-checkpoint` origin. The proof now verifies the new receipt and its owner's prior recovery, including a clean intermediate source. Agent, process, actual terminal, CLI restart and installed/cold readers pass; deeper chains remain open. Category: the repeated-tear proof excluded the new receipt's own recovered Session; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 restarted torn inheritance (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): an owned fork writer is stopped after half its inheritance row and killed. The compiled CLI originally resumed the target but left its checkpoint `unknown`. Startup now checks and temporarily opens the source and reuses the existing proof. The first adaptation recreated a missing source gate, and further CLI reds showed an unenabled resident worker and a one-shot invocation without credentials reconciled before their own admission errors. The recovery-only source open now requires its existing gate, and startup checks those admissions first. Actual CLI, process cuts, interactive switch and installed/cold readers pass; other limitations are in [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md). The first failed fixture output identified an inherited gateway credential source name, without logging a credential value or sending a model request; restart fixtures now explicitly disable gateway inheritance. Category: resume lacked a bound source owner, admission ordering and torn proof; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 resumed CLI fixture (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first restart probe supplied `--user-dir` with `--resume`, which correctly rejected the attempt because resumed settings are pinned. The fixture now isolates the child process home and omits the locked flag. Category: the resume fixture attempted to change a pinned setting; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 repeated recovered-origin torn inheritance (2026-11-06, resolved within bounded scope): a real fork from a Session whose checkpoint was recovered from one torn inheritance itself partially wrote the next inheritance and returned `unknown`. The new host proof checks both facts, their evidence and original receipts with a two-fact limit; the real source and clean-descendant cases, terminal, installed package and process cuts pass. A synthetic candidate checks 64/65 references and a self-cycle; deeper chains and post-recovery-created checkpoint sources remain open. The first type check of added gate tests failed because a mutable Session variable was captured in callbacks; a locally fixed Session reference restored narrowing and `npm run check` passed. Category: a second metadata-only interruption lacked a verified chain to the first; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 installed indirect fixture patch escaping (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a multi-hunk patch again matched a nested generated-program newline using one fewer escape than the file contains, so `apply_patch` rejected it without changing files. Apply the independent hunks and match the nested string's exact source bytes. Category: nested source-string escaping is lost in a patch anchor; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 indirect ordinary-origin torn inheritance (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real fork inherited a valid original checkpoint receipt, then a committed rewind from that fork partially wrote its inheritance row. Reconciliation returned `unknown` because the original receipt owner differed from the immediate source. It now verifies the source's inherited branch and original journal/receipt before and during the one-use append, and repeats those checks for cold readers. A source with a recovered-origin reference remains unknown. Category: direct-owner-only recovery omits a verifiable ordinary inherited source; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 evidence-record patch anchor (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a multi-file documentation patch expected `restore a grant` while the existing current entry said `restore grants`; `apply_patch` rejected it without changing files. Use the exact current text and smaller independent hunks. Category: a documentation patch anchor drifted from the existing sentence; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 post-recovery checkpoint descendant (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after a proven metadata-only torn recovery, a newly captured checkpoint inherited into a real fork as an ordinary direct-origin reference. Changing the original torn file left the fork's new checkpoint `available` instead of `unknown`, even though the source Session's own checkpoint and directory history depend on that evidence. The new receipt now carries a distinct post-recovery origin, and descendants check both its journal commitment and the original recovery evidence. Category: a fresh receipt copied out of a recovered Session loses the source Session's evidence fence; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 empty history with missing torn evidence (2026-11-06, resolved locally): before any new directory snapshot existed, the history getter returned `available` after the recovered torn evidence was removed. The per-receipt checks were never reached for an empty snapshot list. The getter now checks the torn classification before reconstructing even an empty history; the separate path inventory used to perform reconciliation retains its existing bounded behavior. Category: a consumer with no entries skips its per-entry evidence fence; S-1/S-2/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 recovered terminal fixture tool list (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the new compiled terminal read returned `Tool is outside the allow list: read_file` because this older write fixture explicitly allowed only `write_file`. The direct recovery mode now includes `read_file` in its owned fixture settings; no product permission rule changed. Category: a new fixture action was absent from its explicit tool list; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 changing-history fixture narrowing (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a TypeScript assertion narrowed the result of a history getter to `available`, then treated later `unknown` checks as impossible even though the test changed the underlying torn files. Calling the getter through a function for each observation models the changing filesystem state. Category: a test assertion retained a type refinement across an external state change; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 metadata-only torn history continuation (2026-11-06, resolved locally for one direct inheritance tail): after a real committed rewind's direct inheritance short write was reconciled and its directory grant explicitly restored, a real Agent's next prompt failed `Directory backup receipt validation failed` before its model request. The preserved torn file was globally treated as uncertain despite an exact metadata-only recovery fact. Directory history and direct checkpoints now use a proof-based classification, while additional, missing or altered evidence remains unknown. Notification, publication and other torn-history paths remain separate. Category: a global torn fence does not distinguish a proven metadata-only interruption from damaged directory history; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 recovered origin fixture narrowing (2026-11-06, resolved locally): introducing a recovered-origin variant made two existing direct-origin tamper fixtures fail TypeScript checking because they assumed every origin had `receiptDigest`. Both fixtures now assert the actual direct variant before mutating it. Category: a new union variant requires existing consumers to narrow the old case; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 recovered checkpoint lineage removal (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the first lineage design stored its proof as an optional field alongside an ordinary direct origin. Removing only that field from the successor journal made a cold reader return `available` rather than `unknown`. The receipt origin still looked ordinary, so the reader silently lost its dependency on the torn recovery. Recovered origins now have a distinct shape; a missing proof is distinguishable and returns unknown. Category: optional evidence permits a recovered object to be misread as an older unqualified object; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 indirect torn terminal assertion (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the compiled `--directory-witness-torn` driver read the indirect witness but asserted its value only for the direct-source mode. The prior terminal completion therefore did not establish the indirect witness was unknown; the real Agent regression did check it. The driver now asserts `unknown` and absent bytes, and the updated compiled case passes. Category: a consumer executes without checking the relevant result; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 recovered checkpoint descendant (2026-11-06, resolved locally for descendants of one direct torn recovery): a real fork of a Session with a verified direct torn recovery reported the inherited checkpoint as `unknown` rather than `available`. The earlier fence prevented the intermediate torn evidence from being flattened away. A distinct recovered origin now carries the intermediate journal commitment, and each read rechecks its recovery and original evidence. Another torn indirect transfer remains unknown. Category: preserving a recovery dependency prevented a usable successor witness; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 work-record whitespace check (2026-11-06, resolved locally): `git diff --check` found a trailing blank line at the end of four previously modified research records after the new entries were added at their beginnings. Removing those blank lines leaves their content unchanged. Category: an existing worktree diff contains unrelated trailing whitespace; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 recovered torn witness descendant (2026-11-06, resolved locally by preserving unknown): an actual fork of a Session with a newly reconciled torn checkpoint witness inherited that reference as an ordinary direct-origin reference (`available` rather than `unknown`). The new fork carried the original receipt but not the intermediate torn evidence or recovery fact, so later readers had no way to recheck that dependency. The reference is now withheld from descendants until a complete lineage commitment exists. Category: flattening a recovered reference discards the evidence needed to justify its recovery; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 recovered source-prefix fixture (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after deliberately advancing the source Session beyond reconciliation, a tamper test truncated only the final eight bytes of the later suffix, leaving the recorded source prefix intact. The first implementation accepted it, although the source journal itself then had an incomplete tail. Cold verification now accepts later complete rows, but returns unknown for an incomplete suffix or a changed committed prefix. Category: committed prefix integrity and whole-journal torn status are separate facts; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 torn checkpoint inheritance reconciliation (2026-11-06, resolved locally for one direct-source torn inheritance): an actual committed rewind writes half of a `directory_checkpoint_inheritance` row, preserves the full torn journal on reopen and reports its external checkpoint as unknown. The initial direct-source fixture had no owner-bound reconciliation entrypoint (`child.reconcileTornCheckpointInheritance is not a function`). The entrypoint now commits a separately verified recovery fact; cold readers recheck the preserved evidence and origin. The partial row is not treated as committed. Unrelated or insufficient evidence, indirect and descendant lineage, and other torn directory history remain unknown. Category: a metadata-only inheritance interruption lacks a durable, cold-verifiable recovery fact; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 installed acceptance fixture patch (2026-11-06): a multi-hunk patch used a single escaped newline when matching the existing nested template's double escape, so `apply_patch` rejected it without modifying the file. Read the focused source range and apply the matching smaller hunks. Category: a patch anchor did not preserve nested source-string escaping; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 committed rewind old-runtime close failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real successor bound to a separate Runtime while the old Runtime's close threw. `acceptNext` logged and swallowed that failure, adopted the successor, and ran the queued model turn (`1 !== 0`). Committed rewind now requires strict old-runtime cleanup before accepting a separate Runtime; the failure stops and retains the unconfirmed successor lock. Category: a transition treats a failed old-owner cleanup as nonfatal while admitting a new owner; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 installed acceptance fixture escaping (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the offline package reached its new acceptance fixture, but Node rejected the generated `-e` program because a newline escape in the enclosing template became a literal newline inside a quoted JavaScript string. Preserving the escape through both template layers made the installed fixture pass. Category: a generated program receives an unintended newline from its enclosing template; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 committed rewind terminal notice (2026-11-06, resolved locally for acceptance failures): the compiled terminal stopped after the injected accept failure and both cold readers recovered the committed branch, but the post-exit screen showed the old session and no committed-ID notice. The first implementation wrote its notice only inside the alternate-screen transcript, which is cleared on terminal close. The notice is now also written to the restored terminal output; the actual terminal assertion passes. Category: an essential recovery message is rendered in a surface that disappears during the same shutdown; S-1/S-3/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 committed rewind acceptance failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): an actual `/rewind 0 --files` restored the workspace and committed the successor, then successor scheduler attachment threw inside `acceptNext`. The terminal did not identify the committed Session and ran the next queued prompt on the partially accepted owner (`1 !== 0` model calls). The handoff now stops before another turn, strictly cleans known owners, preserves unconfirmed locks and reports the committed ID. Category: a post-commit owner transition reports a local error but permits later work in partially switched state; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 recovered Session gate replacement (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a reopened committed Session's gate was replaced before its recovery failed. `Session.close` correctly kept the old lock but returned without throwing; the fatal handler did not report unconfirmed cleanup. The actual interactive regression failed its cleanup-notice assertion. The handler now checks for a remaining Session lock after close, reports unconfirmed cleanup and retains the existing evidence. Category: a completed close call does not prove the matching lock was removed; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 fatal marker terminal wrapping (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after the shutdown notice fix, the compiled terminal showed the committed ID and full marker-conflict message, but its 120-column screen split `uncertain` across two physical lines. The driver's contiguous-text assertion failed despite the displayed content. Joining physical screen lines for this exact message passes. Category: terminal width wraps a semantic string at a non-word boundary; S-1/S-3/S-6 apply.

W385 earlier committed rewind terminal notices (2026-11-06, resolved locally): actual compiled terminal marker-conflict, inherited-witness short-write and successor-bind recovery-failure fixtures stopped, but their post-exit screens showed only the old Session ID. All three committed-ID notices were lost when the alternate screen closed. They now share the restored-screen notice and stop input before cleanup; all three actual terminal fixtures pass. Category: related consumers share a terminal shutdown delivery boundary; S-1/S-3/S-6 apply.

W385 fatal-bind terminal fixture injection (2026-11-06, resolved locally): its first source-loader rewrite inserted the binding failure before the function opening, so CLI startup failed `session is not defined` before exercising rewind. Inserting that fault inside the binding function reached the intended recovery failure and exposed the missing committed-ID notice. Category: a source-loader injection was placed outside the scope of its referenced parameter; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 proof-regression fixture typing (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the real interleaving fixture passed at runtime, but `npm run check` rejected direct assignment to Node's readonly `fs.lstatSync` type. Installing and restoring the test interceptor through its property descriptor passes the fixture and type check. Category: a runtime-supported test interception conflicts with the declaration's readonly property; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 committed marker proof regression (2026-11-06, resolved locally): during the new scoped recovery, an injected branch truncation immediately after the first durable proof made the second proof uncommitted. The first implementation then followed the generic rollback path and changed the already-restored workspace from `BEFORE` back to `AFTER`. The focused interactive regression failed `AFTER !== BEFORE`. Scoped committed recovery now requires the second proof, stops before rollback and leaves the transaction and tagged staging file intact. Category: a validated committed state falls back to the generic uncommitted branch after a later proof changes; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 committed rewind marker failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally for verified transactions): an actual interactive `/rewind 1 --files` restored the workspace from `AFTER` to `BEFORE` and wrote the branch, but an injected committed-marker rename failure produced no rewind completion; the source Session had already been released. The focused test failed its `Rewound files and conversation` assertion. Recovery now binds the original transaction identity, validates the branch and workspace under the store and Session gates, and resumes the committed Session after strict cleanup and explicit directory regrant. Conflict, altered evidence and cancellation retain an uncertain outcome and stop. Category: the committed workspace transaction fails after its branch is durable but its identity is not propagated to the interactive owner; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 committed rewind inheritance sync failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally for intact history): an actual interactive `/rewind 1 --files` restored workspace bytes and committed its branch, then an injected fsync failure on the inherited checkpoint witness row left the terminal on the released source Session. The terminal now identifies and verifies the committed successor, requires strict runtime cleanup and explicit directory regrant, and continues when the complete journal can be re-synced. Agent, compiled terminal and installed fixtures pass. Category: a post-commit witness failure is reported but the committed interactive owner is not adopted; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 committed witness torn-journal continuation (2026-11-06, resolved locally by stopping): the `torn` stage timed out after reporting `Directory backup receipt validation failed`. Session.open retained the partial inheritance row as `.torn-*`; later directory snapshot receipt validation conservatively treats that evidence as unknown. Recovery now stops with the committed ID before another Agent turn and preserves the torn evidence; source and installed fixtures pass. Reconciliation remains open. Category: an uncertain journal contaminates a later Agent history operation; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 committed witness torn-check typing (2026-11-06): TypeScript did not retain the narrowed optional Session inside the directory-list callback. Copy the file path to a local constant before the callback. Category: a closure loses narrowing of a mutable outer variable; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 committed rewind successor bind failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally for tested paths): real interactive `/rewind 0 --files` restored `AFTER` to `BEFORE` and committed its branch, then an injected successor runtime bind failure left the old released Session as interactive owner. The terminal now requires strict cleanup before a fresh runtime, reopens and requests directory regrant if the successor's authority was retired, and reports the committed Session ID if recovery fails. Seven source fault scenarios, compiled terminal and installed before/after/failure fixtures pass. Category: a post-commit runtime error is handled like a pre-commit command failure; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 nested runtime bind cleanup (2026-11-06, partial): source inspection of the host bind path shows that binding may construct a separate fresh Runtime; its earlier failure handler called ordinary `close`, which can suppress individual resource-close rejections. The failed fresh Runtime now uses strict cleanup and propagates an explicit unconfirmed-cleanup error; a synthetic error fixture confirms committed-rewind recovery stops instead of creating another owner. The actual nested fresh-Runtime cleanup-failure interleaving remains unverified. Category: a failed nested owner is outside the outer owner's cleanup proof; S-2/S-4/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 strict-close callback arity (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the internal close function accepts a strictness flag, so exposing it directly as `Runtime.close` could let a higher-order caller pass an unrelated index as the flag. The public method now uses a zero-argument wrapper and strict closure stays behind `closeForHandoff`; focused checks and offline installation pass. Category: a new optional argument leaks through an existing zero-argument API; S-2/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 rewind source comment placement (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a comment-only patch matched another `let nextRuntime` in session resume. The comment was moved using the surrounding rewind recovery context; no behavior changed. Category: a non-unique patch anchor targeted an adjacent transition; S-1/S-7 apply.

W385 rewind completed-release callback failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real Session/runtime releases its owner and directory grants successfully, then the enclosing release callback throws before returning. Rewind left the old Session open but its runtime released; the expected same-ID recovery was not called (`false !== true`). Runtime now signals completion only after its cleanup succeeds; a callback failure before release leaves the original runtime live, and real Agent, compiled terminal and installed-package recovery pass. A revoked lease alone is not treated as proof of completed cleanup. Category: a completed cleanup is conflated with the containing callback's return; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 release completion callback arity (2026-11-06, resolved locally): adding an optional completion callback to `releaseSession` made its existing use as an `Array.map` callback receive the numeric index as that callback; focused tests failed `completed is not a function` and TypeScript rejected the signature. The call now forwards only the owner. Completion is also withheld when a previously ignored resource cleanup rejects; a real background cleanup failure test passes. Category: passing a multi-argument API directly to a higher-order callback leaks extra arguments; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 cleanup-failure fixture config (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the new real background cleanup test supplied only `features.background`; Session validation and TypeScript require the `tasks` and `subagents` booleans too. The fixture supplies both as false and the resource-failure test passes. Category: a test configuration omitted required schema fields; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 completed-release terminal error display (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the actual terminal recovered the same session and displayed `error: injected completed`, but the driver asserted the entire injected message after the status line truncated it at the terminal width. Waiting for the visible prefix as well as the recovery notice makes the compiled terminal and two cold readers pass. Category: an on-screen status projection was mistaken for its full error text; S-1/S-3/S-6 apply.

W385 committed checkpoint witness receipt (2026-11-06, resolved locally for new captures): an actual writer killed after the `directory_checkpoint_witness` row's append and sync, but before final receipt publication, left two cold opens `unknown` because the receipt existed only in process memory. A complete prepared receipt is now synced before commitment; cold use requires its digest and all original evidence to match, while an uncommitted candidate remains unknown. Source/fork cold readers, compiled terminal writer death and offline installation pass at `582fb993274030510f4a91f51d1834b815bcbe1d3cf476ceae05e7751c89d3f0`. Category: the journal commits a receipt digest before any durable copy of the receipt bytes exists; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 prepared receipt journal fault expectation (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after the new prepared receipt is synced, an injected witness-row fsync failure still fences the writer, but its complete row is visible and a later cold reader can re-sync the journal and verify the prepared receipt. The previous test expected `unknown` on every cold open and failed on `available`. The updated check injects a separate recovery-sync failure, which remains unknown, then checks two successful cold re-syncs and the original bytes. This observation does not establish power-loss persistence. Category: a pre-existing recovery expectation assumed the newly added durable receipt did not exist; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 expansion hook test lease (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the extended interactive suite and focused rerun both failed `expansion hook is raised once before a skill run...` with an empty run list. The fixture's stub Runtime had no `sessionLease`, whereas interactive requires a live bound Session before issuing a human skill capability. A real bound Runtime now supplies the lease and the stub skill consumes the issued capability before recording the run; the focused test and all 26 interactive tests pass. Category: an older execution fixture omits a required owner-lifetime dependency; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 bounded fork checkpoint association (2026-11-06, resolved locally): an actual Agent checkpoints the first turn, completes and checkpoints a second turn, then forks at the first turn. The first checkpoint row was journaled after its turn and was omitted by the history slice (`0 !== 1`), so its external witness could not transfer. Associations now follow their recorded turn boundary, excluding later turns and avoiding duplicate rows. Initial, first and last turn tests plus the compiled conversation-only rewind and two cold readers pass at identity `ca4247442ebd4b44dbe3ffc0e55532273a27fd516986bc88c6d1fa15ae72fc32`. This follows the host file-rewind boundary mapping; source snapshot/fork bodies and the host rewrite are recorded in W385. Category: a post-turn journal row is mistaken for a later-turn association; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 inherited witness target gate (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real fork replaces its target Session gate after the branch row and before inheritance. The first inheritance implementation swallowed the lock failure and returned a live fork (`Missing expected exception`). Both Session locks are now rechecked before unavailable evidence is treated as best effort, including the empty-reference path; source and target replacement cases pass. Category: optional evidence handling masks loss of its owning Session lock; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 inherited checkpoint witness (2026-11-06, resolved locally for recorded transfers): a real Agent captures present and missing external paths, tracks another path later, then forks twice. The inherited checkpoint existed in the successor journal, but inspection reported `unknown` instead of `available` because only the source Session's receipt was recognized. A target-journal fact now binds verified origin receipts; cold readers preserve first-tracked-later uncertainty and reject missing or altered origin evidence. Interrupted transfer reconciliation remains open. Category: branch history copies a checkpoint association without its external witness; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 successor checkpoint path forgery (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the newly added `checkpointPaths` field could be supplied directly to `Session.append` for a fabricated branch; the regression failed `Missing expected exception`. Issuance now reads the live source Session's journal and tracked-path state and consumes a one-use target/content-bound authorization before append; old branch rows remain readable. Direct forgery, mutated content, different target, reuse and source in-memory/journal mismatch pass at identity `d4b8dfd34153b07323a152349a9b2f6217bf3216a6d930d8d944580dddc0de50`. This is a new checkpoint branch transaction, not a rerun of the interrupted lane-binding review. Category: a derived state record is reachable through an unrestricted journal append; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 successor checkpoint terminal driver (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after adding configuration and reload checkpoints, the driver saw the rewind preview's external path and a ready indicator, then asserted a token before the separate apply-token output arrived. The JSON view also showed folded earlier lines, which did not establish that folding hid the token. Waiting explicitly for the token made the three successor terminal checkpoints and six cold readers pass. Category: a ready indicator and partial preview were mistaken for complete command output; S-1/S-3/S-6 apply.

W385 successor checkpoint tracking (2026-11-06, resolved locally for new captures): a real Agent tracks one external path, forks, tracks a second path under the successor grant, and requests a new checkpoint. The first result was `unknown` because the branch carried only unavailable path strings. Branches and rewind proof now carry tracked identities; the successor's current grant is still required. Agent, cold, compiled terminal and installed tests pass at `daff0fc308ccc33c6f3b53e6a7e4c76dadffa5b1978669e7c480780fa81c10ea`; legacy or inconsistent path state stays unknown. Parent historical receipt transfer remains open. Category: branch projection preserves uncertainty but loses identities needed by a later independent capture; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 research diff whitespace (2026-11-06, pre-existing): `git diff --check` on the research files reports an extra blank line at EOF in CURRENT, FEATURES, STATUS and coding-standard.
