The witness updates only changed their earlier content; the existing trailing blank lines are retained. Product/test diff whitespace check passes. Category: pre-existing formatting in unrelated edited ranges; S-1/S-2 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness authorization test typing (2026-11-06): the new test passed at runtime but TypeScript rejected direct access to an optional union result and a captured callback value. Narrow the result with `in` and copy the captured value after assertion before using it in the retry callback. Category: callback assignment and union refinements do not persist automatically; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness commitment mutation (2026-11-06, resolved locally): an actual checkpoint transaction's commit wrapper changes the submitted digest in place. The authorization retained the same object reference, so the altered commitment was appended (`true !== false` for the no-row assertion). Issuance now snapshots the commitment; the actual transaction test checks changed content, a different Session and reuse, and passes at identity `27cf427a8a6226d0ed31dc5d5d64f6d9102dd13ae2f2771da8ace92a9a539335`. This fixture modifies a trusted method to exercise the internal transaction boundary; it does not establish protection against arbitrary process code. Category: a capability and submitted input share mutable state; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness commitment authorization (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after a workspace-only checkpoint, a direct caller could invoke `Session.commitDirectoryCheckpointWitness` without any live bracket and append an arbitrary receipt digest. The original regression failed `Missing expected exception`; issuance now follows group verification and consumption binds the Session and exact commitment. Forged direct calls and real authorized capture pass their regressions at identity `27cf427a8a6226d0ed31dc5d5d64f6d9102dd13ae2f2771da8ace92a9a539335`. This check concerns the new checkpoint transaction and does not revisit the interrupted lane-binding review. Category: a protected journal append is reachable through an unrestricted wrapper; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness FIFO replacement (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a separate cold reader swaps a checked regular receipt name for a FIFO immediately before open. With `O_NONBLOCK` deliberately removed from that open, the child reaches the replacement and times out; the production nonblocking open returns unknown and releases the Session. Metadata and backup reads use nonblocking no-follow descriptors and revalidate identity and bytes. This exercises one named-leaf replacement, not every directory or journal race. Category: a checked regular-file name is replaced by a blocking special file; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint journal descriptor access (2026-11-06, resolved locally): replacing pathname reads with positional reads on the Session journal descriptor made a newly created Session's witness return `unknown`, because new-session journals are opened append/write-only. The two fault-injection tests also did not reach their intended boundaries. An independent nonblocking read-only descriptor now verifies the same inode as the Session's writer; the witness and journal-fault tests pass, while the writer access mode stays unchanged. Category: a write-only owner descriptor is assumed readable; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint FIFO-test typing (2026-11-06): TypeScript rejected `.code` on the child process `Error`; narrow the optional property before comparing the timeout code. Category: unchecked platform error property; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 automatic checkpoint cancellation (2026-11-06, resolved locally): a real Agent completes its turn and enters the asynchronous automatic checkpoint, but the first version used a fresh signal and returned a successful run after cancellation (`Missing expected rejection`). The live turn signal now reaches the completion chain and a committed `turn_end` is not duplicated; the targeted regression passes. Category: an asynchronous completion step outlives its caller signal; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 automatic checkpoint witness journal failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after a real Agent finishes its turn, an injected fsync error while appending the automatic checkpoint's receipt commitment fences the Session, but the first `snapshotBoundary` returned success (`Missing expected rejection`). It now propagates cancellation, Session closure and a fenced journal while retaining ordinary best-effort checkpoint errors; both targeted cases pass. Category: automatic lifecycle hides a fatal Session failure; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness journal failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): an injected fsync failure while appending the receipt commitment fenced the Session, but the first checkpoint wrapper swallowed that failure (`Missing expected rejection`). It now propagates fenced or closed Session failure and leaves the receipt unpublished; ordinary grant loss returns unknown. The real-Agent tests pass. Category: uncertain journal failure is hidden by best-effort witness handling; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint receipt independent binding (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after an actual Agent checkpoint, changing the retained backup in place and changing its SHA-256 in the receipt made a separate cold reader report `available` with forged bytes. A Session-journal commitment now binds the exact receipt bytes before publication; missing, changed or interrupted evidence stays unknown. Source and installed-package cold-reader tamper tests pass. This host transaction is absent from the recovered source. Category: a mutable result and its claimed digest share one unbound record; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 directory backup regression after checkpoint integration (2026-11-06, resolved locally): the combined Agent/interactive suite reported 208 pass, one failure and three skips. The existing `directory backup cannot cross grant revocation or a same-ID Session rebind` test expected two unknown backup records for a missing path and a directory, but observed one. The earlier missing-file implementation writes a dedicated `directory_missing_backup` with a receipted missing history reference; `directoryBackups` enumerates present-file backup rows and sidecars only. The test now checks the actual missing row and selected missing reference, plus the single unknown directory sidecar; the focused real-Agent regression passes. Category: a fixture predates the separate missing-file record; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint witness process-test typing (2026-11-06): the real-process crash fixture passed but `npm run check` rejected its `Session | undefined` variable inside the repeated cold-open loop. Binding a local without an explicit annotation then triggered TS7022 because of the loop reassignment. Annotate the local `Session | undefined` and assert it exists before inspection and close. Category: a loop reassignment loses a type refinement; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 persistent external checkpoint witness (2026-11-06, partially resolved): the initial real-Agent regression lacked checkpoint capture and inspection entrypoints (`undefined` versus `function`). Manual and automatic checkpoints now persist and verify checkpoint-bound external witnesses, and Agent, terminal and offline-installed cold-reader tests pass. Cross-branch witness ownership, interrupted-intent reconciliation and external restore remain open. Category: independently observed file history is not bound to the selected workspace checkpoint; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 association recovery journal failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): `Session.open` previously caught an injected fsync error after writing a recovered checkpoint row and returned a live Session whose in-memory events omitted the visible row. The red regression failed `uncertain recovery must not return a live Session`. The uncertain open now fails and retains the intent; a later open syncs and revalidates the named/open journal before clearing an intent for a matching visible row. A second sync failure retains the intent, and a separate short-write process preserves the torn journal before retry. Source and offline-installed fault tests pass at identity `aff187697b65f8075f4ba2e6f476027f1dd7d269ef743e98241df45654d1be54`. Category: recovery treats an uncertain journal write as a harmless read failure; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 association sync failure (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after the real manifest rename, an injected containing-directory fsync failure previously made `checkpointConversation` delete its association intent although the manifest existed. The red regression failed `0 !== 1`. Capture failures now retain the intent; recovery re-syncs and revalidates the bound manifest, objects and directories, retaining intent if recovery sync also fails. Source tests and installed-process fault/cold readers pass at identity `a74c406d448382441a3f910b9eb85f80f8b84cfe4028b5918b0c175b92aa8745`. Source `Fet`/`Tke`/`Cwt`/`E$n`, sfn1dbxq SHA-256 `4c74137eb23221981404d0ca87b765912f83344851d5c132b21857e5685b03da`, UTF-16 [4731316,4733481)/[4734814,4735645)/[4743506,4743628), has no host manifest or association; this is a host rewrite. Category: a failed durability step after publication is mistaken for no publication; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 research patch context (2026-11-06): one record-only patch used `SIGKILLED` instead of the existing `SIGKILLed`, so its context did not match and no file changed. Subsequent edits use the exact existing text. Category: manual patch context typo; S-1/S-2 apply.

W385 replaced Session gate on recovery (2026-11-05, resolved locally): an actual stopped helper held the original gate after its name was replaced and its Session owner SIGKILLed; same-ID recovery previously acquired the replacement. New Session locks bind the gate device/inode and preserve the lock if that name changes; recovery waits for the original gate after its name is restored. Legacy locks without this identity retain weaker evidence, and same-user rewriting of both lock and gate is not excluded. This is an owned process fixture, not the interrupted lane-binding review. Category: a mutable gate name is mistaken for the originally locked gate; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 gate-replacement probe setup (2026-11-05): the first scratch subprocess fixture failed to parse because its embedded program contained an unescaped newline; no owner or gate test had run. Escaping the nested string produced the separate actual replacement failure above. Category: embedded process-fixture escaping; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 Session gate record consumer (2026-11-05, resolved locally): the first gate-identity implementation passed bracket and legacy Session storage cases but two rewind regressions failed because `checkpoints.ts` read a Session branch lock through its strict checkpoint-lock schema and rejected `gate`. The shared Session schema now serves branch-lock readers, while ordinary checkpoint-lock validation stays strict. Category: lock format changed without updating a second reader; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint branch gate (2026-11-05, resolved locally): a real committed rewind transaction and separate gate holder previously allowed `recoverCheckpoint` to remove the transaction and dead branch lock. Recovery now acquires the Session gate before effects and retains it through cleanup. New transactions carry the gate identity even if the lock is absent; a changed or busy gate retains evidence. Committed, SIGKILL-uncommitted and legacy cases pass. Category: checkpoint recovery bypasses Session gate ownership; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint staging gate (2026-11-05, resolved locally): the initial branch gate adaptation still removed a store staging file before trying the gate. Store cleanup now runs only after transaction recovery has obtained the gate; the busy-gate regression keeps workspace and store staging. Category: outer cleanup runs before the resource owner is fenced; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 checkpoint staging lease (2026-11-05, resolved locally): after moving the store sweep, a contender still acquired the gate during that sweep because transaction recovery had released it. The cleanup now runs under the same lease; the regression observes the contender blocked during the actual unlink. Category: cleanup outlives the ownership lease; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 Session lock publication cleanup (2026-11-05, resolved locally): after a separate process replaces the published lock and gate, the gate check throws but the original failure handler deleted the replacement lock containing `OTHER`. Failure cleanup now checks the stored gate and named/opened lock identity before unlinking; the fixture preserves both objects. The identity check and unlink remain separate operations. Category: failure cleanup trusts a mutable published name; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint helper exit (2026-11-05, resolved locally): after an actual Session bracket reaches ready and its native helper is SIGKILLed, a separate gate descriptor still cannot acquire the lock while the Session is open and its handle is abandoned. The Session previously released its shared gate only on explicit finish, cancel, or owner/grant abort. Close-driven settlement now releases it after the helper exits; a two-helper real-process regression confirms the other live helper keeps the gate. Category: process exit and owner resource cleanup diverge; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint shared gate (2026-11-05, resolved locally): two actual Session brackets started concurrently on different files returned `[ready, unknown]` instead of `[ready, ready]` because they separately locked the same gate. Session now owns one reference-counted lease inherited by both helpers, releases it only after the final helper exits, and excludes duplicate active evidence IDs. Category: per-file resource ownership conflicts with a shared checkpoint boundary; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint Session bracket ownership (2026-11-05, open): the native/TypeScript bracket works with a real Session fixture, but the Session has no owner-bound entrypoint (`undefined` instead of `function`). The low-level adapter relies on its caller for gate ownership and the current-grant callback. Add a Session wrapper that validates the private evidence name and FD, acquires and checks its own gate, and cancels an abandoned handle on owner close; do not treat the result as a durable checkpoint receipt. Category: host primitive missing its Session authority owner; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint bracket descriptor typing (2026-11-05): the first adapter type check fails because Node's `ChildProcess.stdio` declaration has a fixed five-entry tuple although this process deliberately opens control FD 5. Read that descriptor dynamically as `unknown`, check it is a duplex stream, and await a failed spawn's close. Category: native extra-descriptor boundary type; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 checkpoint bracket integration (2026-11-05, open): the owned Session checkpoint regression has no runtime bracket adapter and fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `src/directory-checkpoint-bracket.js`. The independent native scratch prototype holds the path descriptors and backup over a real checkpoint, but it is not installed or connected to Session authority. Port the descriptor protocol into the native reader and bind its one-shot adapter to a live current grant and named root; durable checkpoint association and rewind stay separate. Category: verified prerequisite lacks a product adapter; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint first-tracked ambiguity (2026-11-05, open): two real Agents and separate cold readers show the source-compatible version-1 fallback selects the first later tracked state even when it differs from the earlier workspace checkpoint. A file absent at the checkpoint can first be tracked as present; a file present at the checkpoint can first be tracked as missing. The message selector retains source semantics, but its fallback is not evidence for an unrelated checkpoint. Checkpoint capture must separately witness the path at that boundary or keep it unavailable. Category: later observation mistaken for prior boundary state; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint preview omission (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after an actual Agent first tracked an external file later than a workspace checkpoint, `previewRewind` returned no external-state indication. It now reports tracked paths as unavailable, or unknown when history is unavailable, and binds that projection to the preview token. This reports the existing boundary and does not perform external restore. Category: an unverified file-history consumer omits an incomplete effect; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint preview branch loss (2026-11-06, resolved locally): applying the initial unavailable preview produced a new Session whose preview said `none` because branch copying omitted directory backup journals. The uncertainty now travels through rewind, fork, configuration, reload and relocation without copying authority or inventing backups; older branch records without it remain unknown. Rewind's transaction proof binds the carried projection. Category: branch history loses an unresolved effect; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint preview terminal fixture (2026-11-06): the compiled CLI displayed the unavailable external path, but the new driver tried to read the token from the JSON section after that section had scrolled off the terminal. Read the still-visible `Apply: /rewind ... --apply TOKEN` line used by the existing driver before issuing the apply command. Category: terminal scroll-aware synchronization; S-1/S-3/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 checkpoint association collision (2026-11-06, resolved locally): two real-process cases stop Session B after its association intent is synced but before capture. Session A's same-name manifest either already exists or is created while B is stopped; after B is SIGKILLed, its cold Session previously gained A's checkpoint (`true !== false` in both cases). New manifests bind the association ID, Session and boundary; mismatched and legacy ambiguous intents stay, and an actual published association still recovers. Source and offline-installed process barriers pass at identity `39651c09834d88d2373023d1e3edade7f5230d19510474dc372b03cea8b0f625`. Category: same-name artifact mistaken for an owner-bound effect; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint association missing object (2026-11-06, resolved locally): after a real writer publishes its own manifest and is SIGKILLed before the Session checkpoint row, removing the referenced workspace object previously let cold recovery append the checkpoint. Recovery now validates the complete manifest and every object before association; missing or corrupt objects retain the intent, and restoring them allows recovery. Source and offline-installed process barriers pass at identity `39651c09834d88d2373023d1e3edade7f5230d19510474dc372b03cea8b0f625`. Category: metadata identity mistaken for a complete snapshot; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint descriptor ownership (2026-11-05, open): a real Session grant was revoked, or revoked and regranted, while a scratch checkpoint helper held the inherited directory FD. The helper still reported `stable` because its descriptor and bytes were unchanged. A separately replaced lexical grant root before the helper started produced the same native `stable` status for the old pinned directory. A future host coordinator must independently check the original live grant/incarnation and the named root against its FD on both sides of the bracket; native stability alone is not authority to commit a checkpoint witness. Category: descriptor stability mistaken for current ownership and lexical-root identity; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 retained observation driver cleanup (2026-11-05): the new observation process fixture sent SIGKILL on a failing assertion but did not await the child's close before closing its inherited FDs. Keep the completion promise in the outer scope and await it in cleanup. Category: child process ownership on test failure; S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 research whitespace check (2026-11-05, open): `git diff --check` over tracked product and test changes passes, while the four already-modified research ledgers report pre-existing added blank lines at EOF. The current edits target their leading or interior sections, so the unrelated existing trailing text is preserved. Untracked files are outside `git diff --check`. Category: unrelated dirty-tree formatting; S-2/S-6 apply.

W385 retained removal capture races (2026-11-05, open): two instrumented real-process probes expose the unconnected quarantine helper. Replacing its `held` name after capture makes mismatch handling move the unrelated `OTHER` file onto the public target; the assertion that the target remain absent fails `true !== false`. Creating `held` before capture makes plain `renameat` overwrite that unrelated file and report success (`0` where nonzero was expected). Use an exclusive capture and preserve every mismatched capture in the transaction directory for later revalidation. Public targets can remain absent after an uncertain capture; that is not a completed removal or a rollback. Category: mutable private names trusted across a capture and recovery boundary; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 retained object current observation (2026-11-05, open): an actual Agent retained publication has a durable historical held-inode record, but the Session exposes no current-object observation (`typeof observeRetainedDirectoryPublication` is `undefined`, expected `function`). Its held file can be moved, exchanged or modified after return, so that historical record must not authorize a later effect. Reopen under the live grant, compare the retained descriptor and name to the publication-time witness and independent backup, and return an observation only for the checked moment. Category: historical receipt mistaken for a current object witness; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 existing-target publication race (2026-11-05, open): an instrumented actual native writer passes its final target recheck, then another process renames the approved existing file and creates `OTHER` at the target. The current `renameat` replaces `OTHER` with `new` and reports success. The separately moved original survives only because the fixture moved it; the later replacement is lost. `W385_DIRECTORY_PUBLISH_RACE_PROBE=1` reproduces `'new' !== 'OTHER'`. The default external Write remains gated. Investigate a descriptor-relative retained capture and exclusive publication with independently verified original bytes before enabling any consumer. Category: final path recheck is not conditional publication; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 retained-replacement experiment (2026-11-05, open): an owned Darwin prototype preserves a late replacement when capture detects a mismatched inode, but an actor replacing the private `staged` name after its final check makes the helper publish `BAD` before its postcheck reports unknown. Replacing the private `held` name before validation also makes the prototype restore `OTHER` to the public target while the original remains under the actor's alternate name. A previously opened writer modifies the retained original after a successful return. Exclusive rename prevents overwrite of an occupied target but does not bind the renamed source name or make a held inode immutable. Investigate publication from an already opened descriptor and preserve uncertain objects; keep this prototype unconnected. Category: private staging and capture path remain mutable; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 descriptor-clone in-place mutation (2026-11-05, open): the descriptor-origin clone prototype's staged inode was changed from `NEW` to `BAD` after its last check, with original size, inode, mode and mtime restored. The clone published `BAD` and reported durable because its postcheck compared only the now-mutated staged descriptor with the new clone and ignored the original ctime. The retained original stayed `ORIGINAL`. Check the staged descriptor against its initial ctime, test a second barrier after that check, and retain all uncertain objects; this scratch experiment stays unconnected. Category: mutable descriptor mistaken for immutable proposed bytes; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 descriptor-clone later mutation (2026-11-05, open): adding staged ctime checks stops the first in-place rewrite, while a rewrite after the final check still publishes `BAD` and is detected only afterward as unknown. The prototype now retains mismatched captured objects rather than auto-restoring a possibly replaced private name. Verify whether the clone's output pathname can be exchanged for an identical file before the first output descriptor is opened; a durable result must identify the object actually published, not merely equal content. Category: check-to-effect and output identity gap; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 clone output identity (2026-11-05, open): after `fclonefileat` created the new target, a real process moved that inode aside and placed another same-content, same-mode file at the target before the helper opened it. The prototype compared the replacement with the staged content and incorrectly returned durable (`0 !== 1`). `fclonefileat` returns no FD for the created inode; pathname observation alone cannot prove that the observed inode was the clone. Keep this variant unconnected and test exclusive rename from a pinned staged descriptor, whose postcheck can compare inode identity, while retaining every displaced object and treating path-source interference as unknown. Category: output content equality mistaken for output identity; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 retained publication Session binding (2026-11-05, open): a real Agent fixture requests the new retained strategy through the existing one-use Session publication capability. The call still takes the older publisher path and its result has no held-object witness, so the fixture's actual tool result is an error. The independent native helper and adapter pass their own process cases but are not yet bound to the Session's backup FD, intent, result or cold receipt. Add an explicit host strategy without changing default external Write, validate its backup descriptor and preserve a receipt-bound held observation; do not treat that observation as immutable current state. Category: native prerequisite lacks Session receipt consumer; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 retained Session fixture typing (2026-11-05): the real Agent test passed after adding the new transaction path, but `npm run check` caught the fixture reading retained-only fields from a union that also contains legacy durable publications. Narrow on the `held` discriminator before inspecting the transaction ID and observation. Category: fixture union narrowing; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 retained owner-death fixture (2026-11-05): the first expanded owner-death test reached an `ENOENT` when it expected the public target after a retained capture barrier. The helper's status pipe belongs to the killed owner, so a post-barrier status write can stop it before publication; at that point the original is expected under the retained transaction directory. Check captured and published barriers separately, assert the actual target absence/presence and retained bytes, and keep both cold results unknown. Category: process-death status-pipe ordering; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 branch Read witness loss (2026-11-05, open): two separate compiled PTY runs perform an actual approved external Read, then either `/thinking on` or a turn-1 workspace `/rewind`. Both successor sessions retain the original bytes after the directory is removed, and the next owned-loopback model request receives those bytes. The configuration branch drops the source observation; the rewind branch converts `tool_result` to an observation without its grant witness. Preserve only a witness rebound to a verified live successor grant, and retain the original witness when no valid transfer is possible so later projection and cold open redact it. Check reload and relocation consumers separately. Category: branch history and authority lifecycle diverge; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. This is not the interrupted lane-binding review.

W385 branch regression fixtures (2026-11-05): the new relocation Agent test stopped at schema validation because its fixture omitted required `features.tasks` and `features.subagents`; no relocation had run. The existing relocation append-failure test also expected an empty target directory, but the current lock protocol deliberately retains one permanent `.jsonl.lock.gate` while deleting the partial journal and releasing its lock. Supply both fixture fields and assert the retained gate explicitly. Category: fixture schema and stale lock-protocol expectation; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 branch PTY fixture cleanup (2026-11-05): successful new configuration/reload/rewind history modes left their owned temporary directories because the existing `finally` cleanup only selected older mode flags. Add the new mode to the same cleanup condition; prior failed fixtures remain in the job's temporary evidence directory. Category: new driver mode omitted from cleanup consumer; S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 post-release restore rollback (2026-11-05, open): a real Agent and runtime release, then an injected rewind-journal failure after a workspace file was restored, leave the original file bytes correctly rolled back and no pending checkpoint transaction. The recovery callback is never invoked because the prepared branch is discarded inside `restoreRewindBranch` before the caller can use its retained grant. Keep that branch available to the existing same-ID recovery only for a non-fenced, uncommitted and fully rolled-back transaction. A committed history proof or remaining transaction must retain its existing reconciliation path. Category: failed owner transition after completed rollback; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 post-release stale rewind (2026-11-05, open): after an actual Session and runtime release, a workspace change makes the final preview stale. `applyRewindAfterRelease` rejects without restoring files, but the same Session's next real Agent Read of its previously approved directory returns `path escapes the workspace (absolute paths are not allowed)` rather than the fixture bytes. The old runtime and directory authority were already released; keeping the original Session open is not enough to continue that grant. The opt-in `W385_REWIND_FAILURE_PROBE=1` records the failure. Map source and host authority transfer, runtime rebinding and changes during shutdown before a fix; no lane-binding review is retried. Category: failed owner transition; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 restored-session Read fixture (2026-11-05): the first recovery implementation preserved the earlier Read and the next unchanged Read correctly returned `Wasted call — file unchanged since your last Read`, so expecting the file bytes again failed. Change the owned external fixture and its mtime before the second real Read; assert the preserved earlier content separately. Category: valid cache fast path in a recovery test; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 recovery fork history (2026-11-05, open): after two real same-ID recovery handoffs the active Session projects both earlier approved Reads, but a normal `forkSession` redacts both because its witness transfer compares only the current incarnation and does not recognize a live recovery continuation. Reuse the same owner/root/incarnation validation as the active projection when carrying a witness into a fork; revoke or regrant must still invalidate the old continuation. Category: unsynchronized history consumer; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 changed-permission recovery (2026-11-05, open): during actual runtime release, an approved directory is removed and re-added, leaving the same final permission entry. The rewind correctly rejects the changed permission state, but it does not invoke the recovery callback and the old Session stays bound to a released runtime. Resume the same journal with current permission state and no carried pre-release grants or Read continuations; a subsequent explicit new grant may open a fresh descriptor but must not reveal the older Read. Category: interrupted authority transition; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 child rewind recovery compatibility (2026-11-05): an actual child Session with a valid explicit workspace checkpoint failed a previously supported rewind before releasing its owner because the new optional same-ID recovery preparation rejected child owners. Treat recovery preparation as unavailable for child or inherited owners while preserving their existing workspace rewind path; do not synthesize child root authority. Category: optional host recovery gate applied to existing path; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 changed-permission PTY fixture (2026-11-05): the first interactive recovery reached the explicit remove/add successfully, but the driver timed out because it counted two `Added` lines in the visible screen and the original line had scrolled away. Wait for an `Added` acknowledgement after the latest `/add-dir` command instead. The post-regrant Read had not yet run. Category: terminal scroll-aware synchronization; S-1/S-3/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 recovery regression edit (2026-11-05): a combined test patch omitted an existing assertion on the same line, so no changes were applied. Use separate exact-context hunks. Category: patch context mismatch; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 same-ID gate fixture (2026-11-05): the first added control stopped before taking a Session lock because `defaultConfig` supplied an empty model and Session schema validation rejected it. Supply the same nonempty fixture model used by the existing process controls, then repeat the gate experiment. Category: fixture configuration; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 stale-rewind evidence edit (2026-11-05): the first documentation patch misspelled the existing `/rewind` context and applied no change. Read the exact line and reapply before continuing. Category: patch context error; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 rewind authority CLI fixture (2026-11-05): two PTY attempts timed out because `/rewind` reported no associated checkpoint; neither reached the post-rewind Read. The first diagnosis attributed this to matching an echoed command, but inspecting the saved journal and script showed the checkpoint block was accidentally inserted in the separate directory-switch branch and never ran. Move it to the directory-rewind branch and wait for the checkpoint result's `checkpoint_digest`. Category: ambiguous fixture patch context and premature diagnosis; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 rewind authority transfer (2026-11-05, open): after the fixture correction, actual PTY `/add-dir`, checkpoint, Read, `/rewind 0 --files` preview/apply and a second Read return `read_file: directory grant is no longer available`. Interactive releases the old runtime before `applyRewind`; runtime release closes the old Session's directory FDs, so the subsequent `carryPermissions` has no live source descriptor to duplicate. Keep old workers quiesced before file effects and preserve only a descriptor duplicated while the prior Session grant was live; reject permission changes or failed handoff. Category: owner transition ordering; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. This product defect is not the interrupted lane-binding review.

W385 rewind descriptor-copy failure (2026-11-05): injecting failure when duplicating the live directory FD makes the first handoff implementation complete instead of rejecting; `copyDirectoryHandles` deliberately records an unavailable grant without throwing. Compare every successor-retained live source grant to its independently owned duplicate before releasing the old runtime, and reject a failed or unexpected copy. Category: silent partial handoff; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 rewind branch identity failure (2026-11-05): a real prepared Session is opened, then an injected `lstat` failure occurs before its identity is saved. The operation rejects but the prepared Session remains open and holds its resources. Close that Session on identity-observation failure; retain its journal when the original inode was not established, rather than guessing which pathname to remove. Category: pre-transaction resource cleanup; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 branch-identity fixture typing (2026-11-05): the behavioral fault test passed, but `npm run check` rejected direct assignment to the Node types' read-only `fs.lstatSync` property. Replace the property through its saved descriptor in the fixture and restore that exact descriptor afterward. Category: test boundary typing; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 patch-format check (2026-11-05): `git diff --check` over the touched set reports pre-existing added blank lines at the ends of `CURRENT.md`, `FEATURES.md` and `STATUS.md`; this slice edited their opening sections only. The focused product/driver diff passes. Preserve the unrelated existing document edits. Category: inherited worktree formatting; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 rewind reference selection (2026-11-05, open): a real Agent with two user turns records an empty earlier snapshot and first tracks an existing file and a missing file in the later snapshot. The requested message-UUID selector is absent (`typeof` is `undefined` instead of `function`). Adapt the recovered last-snapshot and version-1 fallback; preserve an earlier unknown candidate instead of treating a later version as proof of the first observation. Category: missing snapshot consumer and uncertain evidence; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md#directory-grant-write-probe-2026-11-05-open).

W385 first missing leaf (2026-11-05, open): an actual Agent Write to a nonexistent leaf in an approved directory leaves the first user snapshot without the path (`undefined` instead of missing version 1 with the parent device/inode). The current backup stream creates a provisional sidecar and fails to open the absent source; its failure does not distinguish a missing leaf from other errors. The in-progress fix uses an owner-bound native parent probe and separately receipted missing witness, retaining unknown when evidence is incomplete. Category: missing source branch and provenance; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md#directory-grant-write-probe-2026-11-05-open).

W385 missing receipt first implementation (2026-11-05): the live receipt check fenced the Session because it used object identity to find an event that `append` stores via `structuredClone`; the failure predicate also failed TypeScript narrowing. Match the unique event ID and serialized event, and narrow failure variants explicitly. Category: journal ownership representation and type narrowing; S-1/S-4/S-6 apply.

W385 missing publication race (2026-11-05, open): the actual instrumented native writer paused after observing a missing target; a competing owned fixture then created `OTHER` at the leaf. The helper returned success and replaced it with `new` because `renameat` overwrites after the last check. The product's default external mutation remains closed. An atomic no-replace publication for the absent case is in progress; failed or interrupted attempts remain unknown. Category: check-to-publication race and host overwrite contract; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 package verification scheduling (2026-11-05): the offline package driver runs `npm run build` in the shared project, which removes and recreates `dist`; concurrently launched CLI and owner-gate consumers failed `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `dist/session.js`. The package itself passed. Wait for the package build to finish before running those consumers. Category: shared build artifact ownership; S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 missing Session publication (2026-11-05, open): an actual Agent fixture with an absent external leaf reaches its confirmed missing witness, but the existing Session publication rejects the final Write with `Directory publication authorization invalid` because the capability currently requires `overwrite: true` and only accepts present-file backup observations. Extend the typed expected state and per-kind authorization without weakening present-file checks. Category: incomplete consumer and input-state mapping; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 missing Session typing (2026-11-05): the first implementation passed the three behavioral probes but `npm run check` rejected property access because `findLast` inferred the whole Session event union. A type predicate constrained to the checked missing-backup discriminant fixes the call boundary. Category: event-union narrowing; S-1/S-6 apply.

W385 missing publication mode witness (2026-11-05, open): changing an absent-target intent's creation mode and the result observation, then recomputing the result and publication receipt, still makes a cold reader report `durable`. The selected mode existed only inside that transaction and lacked an independent earlier witness. Persist the sampled creation mode with the separately receipted missing observation and require the later intent to match; legacy missing observations without it remain readable as history but are not enough to authorize creation. Category: incomplete durable provenance; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 missing commit-barrier fixture (2026-11-05): the combined test patch missed an escaped regular-expression context and applied no edits; the following name-filtered test command matched no test despite exit 0. Split the patch into exact hunks and require the intended test name in the result. Category: partial patch context and vacuous name filtering; S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 external rewind (2026-11-05, open): an actual Agent fixture uses the Session missing-target transaction to create an external file after a turn-0 workspace checkpoint. `previewRewind(session, 0)` contains no external remove operation (`undefined` versus the expected path/action). The source selects a user-message snapshot while the host associates completed turns with workspace checkpoints; their alignment, descriptor-bound removal and mixed-effects recovery must be mapped before enabling this red probe. Category: incomplete restore consumer and boundary mapping; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. This is a concrete product gap, not a retry of the interrupted lane-binding review.

W385 descriptor-relative removal (2026-11-05, open): the owned production-helper fixture invoking `--remove` with a current file/parent frame exits 1 with `error not_renamed 22`; no native removal consumer exists yet. The in-progress adapter will check single-link current identity and parent before unlink and retain unknown after a commit attempt. Category: missing restoration primitive; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 removal parent rebinding (2026-11-05, open): the first descriptor-relative removal helper held the old parent FD after that directory was renamed and a different parent created at its original name. At the pre-recheck barrier it returned 0 instead of refusing; the held descriptor alone did not prove the lexical parent still selected it. Reopen each parent component from the grant FD immediately before the final target check and compare the reopened parent identity. The final recheck-to-unlink window remains non-atomic. Category: path-to-descriptor rebinding; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 removal final race (2026-11-05, open): the opt-in `W385_DIRECTORY_REMOVE_RACE_PROBE=1` holds the native helper after its final target recheck, renames the original file and creates `OTHER` at its former name. On release the helper removes `OTHER`; the assertion that the replacement survives fails (`false !== true`). The primitive is not connected to Session or `/rewind`; a user-visible deletion path must not be reported as identity-safe on this evidence. Source `kVr` likewise uses pathname unlink after checking; resolve the host concurrency contract and recoverable ownership before integration. Category: recheck-to-unlink race; S-1/S-2/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 removal command dispatch (2026-11-05): adding `--remove` as a three-argument native command made a normal write to a file literally named `--remove` fail, because its existing write arguments matched the new command shape. Add a distinct terminal operation marker and update both adapter and driver; retain a real ordinary-file regression. Category: ambiguous internal protocol and consumer synchronization; S-1/S-2/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 removal API research (2026-11-05): local `man 2 unlink` and `man 2 rename` pipelines did not complete within 10 seconds and were stopped. They yielded no usable API evidence; the observed delete race remains open. Category: local reference lookup unavailable; no product change or source-parity conclusion.

W385 quarantine metadata probe (2026-11-05, open): on the owned Darwin fixture, moving an unchanged file into a same-volume quarantine changes its ctime. A separate actor then overwrote the same inode with equal-length different bytes and restored mtime before the move; dev/ino, mode, size and mtime still matched while the SHA-256 differed. Comparing post-rename ctime to the original would reject the unchanged control, while omitting ctime would accept changed content. A future removal decision must bind and revalidate content or preserve the object for recovery; inode matching alone does not settle the defect. Category: mutation-masked provenance; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-7 apply.

W385 quarantine final race (2026-11-05, open): the owned fixture captures the expected file in a 0700 directory and validates an independent byte witness, then pauses after its last private-name check. An actor with the same directory access renames the held inode and replaces that private name; releasing the helper deletes the replacement. Cleanup fails because the original is still present and the outcome is not completed, but the replacement was already removed. Quarantine protects the demonstrated public-name exchange and preserves conflicts but does not provide a conditional unlink against same-owner private-directory interference. Category: moved check-to-effect race; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-7 apply. Product removal remains unconnected.

W385 removal private-name race (2026-11-05, open): after adapting the actual unconnected native helper to quarantine and compare independently supplied bytes, the original public-name replacement regression passes, but `W385_DIRECTORY_REMOVE_PRIVATE_RACE_PROBE=1` still reproduces deletion of a replacement inserted inside the quarantine after its final check. The caller-known transaction ID and original held inode remain discoverable, but no identity-conditional unlink is proved. Keep Session and `/rewind` disconnected and preserve this red test. Category: private namespace check-to-unlink race; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-7 apply.

W385 removal reoccupied target (2026-11-05, open): after quarantine and byte verification, an owned barrier creates a new file at the original name before unlinking the captured inode. The new file survives, but the helper returns success even though the name is present at completion. Recheck the lexical parent and destination after unlink before emitting durable; a changed name/parent must remain unknown. A change after that final check is still outside this observation. Category: incomplete effect-to-result validation; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 removal prior-writer descriptor race (2026-11-05, open): after an exact captured-file/byte check, an actor holding a writable descriptor from before quarantine writes `MUTATED!` and syncs while the helper is paused at its last pre-unlink barrier. The helper removes the last quarantine link; `W385_DIRECTORY_REMOVE_OPEN_FD_RACE_PROBE=1` fails because the modified file is no longer linked. A private directory alone does not isolate an already-open writer. The Darwin SDK declares `F_SETLEASE`, but its public headers found here do not define the lease modes or establish how it treats prior descriptors; no lease guarantee is inferred. Category: open-descriptor check-to-unlink race; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-7 apply.

W385 quarantine status ordering (2026-11-05, open): after changing the helper to retain rather than unlink, an actor writes through a pre-existing descriptor while the helper is paused immediately after its `quarantined` status. The modified bytes remain linked, but the helper exits 0 with the earlier observation. Recheck held inode, parent and destination after that status; a changed observation appends an unknown result. This narrows the ordering gap but cannot make bytes immutable after the helper returns. Category: stale result observation; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6 apply.

W385 quarantine cleanup evidence (2026-11-05, open): after capturing a replacement directory or symlink and restoring it to its original name, the helper automatically deletes the now-empty transaction directory. The owned fixture expecting retained transaction evidence fails because the directory is absent. Since the directory name itself can be exchanged by a same-owner actor, even automatic empty-directory removal is another unchecked pathname effect. Retain a created transaction directory on every outcome and classify unsuccessful attempts after creation as unknown until owner-bound reconciliation exists. Category: evidence cleanup and directory-name ownership; S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W385 removal UUID casing (2026-11-05): Zod accepts a valid uppercase UUID, but the native quarantine validator initially admitted only lowercase hex and returned `not_removed`.
