Source `yk` filters ordinary mode asks, while `_Wo` can re-enter the full pipeline for a PreToolUse ask and has no equality exemption. The duplicate host prompt is reproduced, but complete coordinator behavior remains open. Fresh independent review is required. Existing S-1, S-4, S-5, S-6 and S-7 apply.

W385 interactive reprompt follow-up (2026-10-30). The first resident two-turn regression changed `session.config.hooks` after startup; the second turn failed the runtime's pinned-configuration check before exercising the permission path. Defining the hook at startup and changing an owned fixture marker between turns preserves the original closed-input control and exercises the rewritten rule ask. Two searches against minified or long single-line text produced oversized tool results; subsequent source checks used bounded UTF-16 slices. An attempted search also named two absent CLI drivers. Two documentation edits supplied identical old and new text and changed nothing; the intended baseline note was edited using its exact anchor. An initial source-producer range included its separator comma; the recorded function ranges and hashes were corrected. The first design reader terminated on an upstream API budget error without a verdict; a fresh bounded review is required. Existing S-1, S-4, S-5, S-6 and S-7 apply.

W385 interactive hook scope (2026-10-30). The earlier hook-rewrite repair was derived from `sxr` and tested through a no-prompt CLI, but its guard was applied to every Agent. An independent source trace found the separate interactive `runHooks` branch, which can reprompt on a rewritten rule ask. The existing interactive CLI baseline confirms the host currently denies that case. The new `--desired` CLI acceptance fails first at rewritten `ask_user_question` because the model receives a rule-denial result before the expected prompt; later desired cases are not reached. The no-prompt review remains valid in its stated scope; interactive parity needs a separate source-backed owner and regression. Existing S-1, S-4, S-6 and S-7 apply.

W385 notebook read handoff (2026-10-30). Deterministic owned replacements between the compiled notebook tools' `stat` and pathname read make read return the replacement notebook and make edit modify that replacement. The original remains separately visible in each fixture. The pending approval-to-use work must also bind notebook bytes to the opened file; this is a tool-level interleaving, not an original-sample runtime observation. Existing S-1, S-4 and S-5 apply. See [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md).

W385 atomic recovery path (2026-10-30). An owned deterministic interleaving moves the directory immediately after the compiled writer closes its complete staged file. Its subsequent directory identity check refuses publication, but the error still names the old temporary path; that pathname is absent and the bytes remain in the moved directory. The protected fixture is unchanged. The source/host publication and recoverable-path contract needs verification before wiring the approval pin; existing S-1, S-4 and S-5 apply. See [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md).

W385 interaction approval fixture (2026-10-30). The first scratch Agent driver used top-level await outside an ES module boundary, so `tsx` stopped before a case ran. After moving the body into an async function, the next attempt stopped during session creation because the feature object omitted required `tasks` and `subagents` flags. Adding explicit false values allowed all twelve cases to run. The first classic-hook scratch module lacked the closing object brace and failed parsing before a hook ran; the corrected module ran all six cases. Its first cleanup attempt had not started because module loading failed, so that owned fixture was removed after inspection. A subsequent search named an absent shell glob, and a second search named an absent identity script; focused existing paths located the prompts and `scripts/identity.mjs`. A second reader was inadvertently started while the first reader was active and was immediately stopped; its partial work is not used. An attempted log insertion matched a duplicated heading and changed nothing; anchoring the existing entry fixed the edit. Existing S-1, S-4 and the one-reader workflow apply.

W385 evidence lookup (2026-10-30). A shell search for an existing producer citation had an unclosed regular-expression group and returned no matches. A later literal search located the existing W385 entries; no source or product file changed. Existing S-1 applies.

W385 hook rewrite candidate (2026-10-30). An initial `floor:rule` guard denied every rewritten input still matching an explicit ask. The new different-path regression passed, but the existing replacement-character hook test failed because its tool-wide `write_file` ask matches both the original and replacement and had previously authorized the rewrite. The initial guard was removed while its rule scope was traced. Source `lc -> S -> qx -> P` shows that an exact matching whole-tool ask remains an ask on recheck; `sxr` converts that returned ask to denial. The prior fixture now uses a mode-based ask and retains its exact replacement, hook and direct-execution assertions; a new separate whole-tool case checks the source-derived denial. The rule-floor guard was restored, and 36 related plus 70 Agent/classic-hook tests, check/build and six actual compiled CLI cases pass. Independent review is pending. Existing S-1, S-2, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 reader scheduling (2026-10-30). A second source reader was started while the plan repair reviewer was still active, contrary to the package's one-bounded-reader workflow. It was immediately stopped and its partial work is not counted. Source investigation continues on the main thread while the independent review finishes. Existing S-4 and the one-active-reader workflow apply.

W385 capability-gate follow-up (2026-10-30). The macOS binding fixture previously checked the cached capability state after `vpe` without explicitly invoking `wGn`; ordinary `vpe` uses `lstat`, so the state could still be `untried`. The earlier statement that the guard performed the probe was incorrect. The fixture now invokes the copied capability probe before interpreting its result. Existing S-1, S-4 and S-5 apply.

W385 plan-rule fixture (2026-10-30). The first rooted-rule regression placed the child as a sibling of the parent, so `path.relative` produced `../child` and the anchored pattern did not represent the intended source-layer root. Moving the owned child beneath the parent makes both a nonmatching parent-root pattern and a matching parent-root pattern meaningful. The initial relative-plan assertion failed before the product repair; the corrected group passes afterward. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 diff rendering follow-up (2026-10-30). The first owned rendering probe expected a denied target to be visible but the fullscreen panel initially used session mode, where the alias deletion was categorized as pre-session; the separate direct protected path was hidden. Instrumenting the existing denial callback and switching the mounted panel to uncommitted mode showed the alias row and its Git baseline deletion. The inline dialog displayed the same deletion after selecting the actual third row. Neither screen displayed the protected fixture text. A shell search with a nonexistent glob also failed before searching; later searches used existing paths. Source local `WKn` and turn `Ie` do not set `isRestricted`, while the remote `pe` conversion does, so the inline callback alone is not evidence for a local source behavior change. An initial log edit matched a duplicate heading and made no change; the entry now anchors to its neighboring item. An initial digest query cut two source declarations across adjacent boundaries; the final source record uses the exact inspected intervals. A later lookup also named nonexistent `test/plan-mode.test.ts`; `rg --files` is required before follow-up searches. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 diff host lookup (2026-10-30). A search named nonexistent `src/tui-terminal.ts`; the actual full-screen host is `src/tui.ts`, and the search returned its existing binding locations. No product file changed. Existing S-1 applies.

W385 diff permission workspace probe (2026-10-30). The first scratch fixture assumed a new Session retained the supplied `ruleWorkspace`, but session initialization seeds the live owner with the session workspace, so the intended mismatch was not present. A second attempt changed the inherited owner but passed the session's separate updates to the direct check, again measuring the wrong owner. The corrected fixture explicitly sets the live update's rule workspace to the parent; its real inline and panel denial callbacks then both return false for an alias whose child-workspace landing is denied, while an explicit child-workspace permission decision denies. A further fixture uses actual `relocateSession` with `sourcePlanTools:true`; `carryForkPermissions` retains the parent rule workspace and both real diff callbacks again miss the child link's denied landing. The first relocation attempt omitted required `features.tasks` and `features.subagents` flags and failed schema validation before the test; adding explicit false values made it run. This establishes the runtime owner-state path, not that a rendered diff will contain the protected file. Existing S-1, S-4 and S-5 apply.

W385 review follow-up lookup (2026-10-30). A search included a nonexistent `test/diff-panel.test.ts`; `rg --files test` located the existing inline and dialog tests. The source lookups completed for their other named files and no edit was made by the search. Existing S-1 applies.

W385 read-lane citation (2026-10-30). An initial note estimated the end of the `t8e` wrapper as 5137. Locating the next declaration and hashing the full body corrected the UTF-16 half-open interval to [5095,5135). Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 checkpoint lookup (2026-10-30). A static search named a nonexistent `src/checkpoint.ts`; the actual `Session.touchFile` and history events are in `src/session.ts`, while rewind uses `src/rewind.ts`. No files changed. Subsequent lookups use existing paths. Existing S-1 and S-6 apply.

W385 candidate review interruption (2026-10-30). The independent path-observation reader did not return a verdict after multiple requests to close its scope and was stopped. Its partial transcript contains no final report, so no review result is counted; a fresh bounded reader must verify the candidate before commit. Existing S-5 and the independent-review gate apply.

W385 read-identity composition (2026-10-30). The first composition test accessed the optional source screenshot alternate before narrowing it; the runtime fixture passed but TypeScript reported TS18048 on four accesses. An explicit `assert.ok` now narrows that branch, and the two binding tests and check pass. The existing source bodies are unchanged. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 review scheduling follow-up (2026-10-30). A second reader was started while the current candidate reviewer was active, despite the one-reader workflow. It was stopped immediately and no result is counted. Existing S-4 and project WORKFLOW section 6 apply.

W385 descriptor-relative experiment (2026-10-30). A macOS `/dev/fd/<directory-fd>/file` probe returned `ENOENT`, so the descriptor path did not provide relative file operations. A separate owned C fixture staged with `openat` and published with `renameat` through a held directory descriptor after replacing its pathname; it changed the moved approved directory and retained the replacement directory. The first compilation failed because Clang could not infer that the error helper exits; marking the helper `_Noreturn` allowed the strict build and probe to pass. This is syscall evidence only, not a product fix or a cross-platform result. Existing S-1, S-4 and S-5 apply.

W385 approval-to-use acceptance (2026-10-30). The compiled driver without `--baseline` fails at `read_file/retarget`: the tool returns the protected fixture rather than an error. Its finally block closes the owned server and removes the fixture. This confirms the source decision adapter does not yet bind the approved path to later file use; the 18-case baseline remains a reproduction rather than an acceptance pass. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 unresolved read path (2026-10-30). The first `di` adapter allowed an unresolved ancestor loop for a read-only tool when no policy was configured. Source `xv` returns `Al` for that observation before later allow paths. A focused regression reproduced the changed verdict; read, search, list and readPaths consumers now apply the copied unresolved-path denial while preserving prior explicit denials. The three related suites pass 33/33 and check passes. Existing S-1, S-2, S-5 and S-6 apply.

W385 peer-file execution workspace (2026-10-30). The first cross-workspace test used the same relative filename on both sides and therefore passed even with the wrong rule workspace. Replacing it with a child-only ancestor link to a denied target reproduced the missed precheck. Passing the execution context's workspace to both existing Read checks now makes the test reject before staging or sending. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-6 apply.

W385 path-observation consumers (2026-10-30). A caller audit found that search and list use the same content-rule matcher but were omitted from the memoized source-observation set. Their execution-workspace cases passed through the matcher's fallback, but each rule bucket could observe separately. Both consumers now share the same per-decision observation; direct deny controls cover their child workspace. Existing S-4, S-5 and S-6 apply.

W385 path-observation ordering (2026-10-30). The initial candidate resolved a path before checking a bare tool denial, so a NUL-containing input returned a path error instead of the existing rule-denial reason. A focused regression reproduced the change; the observation is now memoized and taken lazily after earlier rule gates. The 30 focused permission/leaf tests and check pass. Existing S-1, S-2 and S-5 apply.

W385 compiled invalid-name control (2026-10-30). The first CLI run assumed the three file tools share an execution-time replacement-character error. NotebookEdit without extra directory grants uses its existing notebook-specific `confine` function and returned `path does not exist` for the missing fixture instead. The driver now asserts the actual notebook error and still checks that no invalid-named file is created; text tools retain their replacement-character assertion. No execution-time path behavior was changed. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 restrictive spelling compatibility (2026-10-30). The first source `di` integration used only source spellings for all policy buckets; an added two-link regression then allowed a path that the host's intermediate-spelling deny had rejected. All policy buckets now also use the host walk's extra spellings: after the restrictive fix, a second regression showed that an allow covering only the source's requested and landing names would otherwise pass where the old host required the intermediate name too. An initial `Omit` over the observation union erased its discriminator and made check fail; a separate source-observation type preserves both branches. Another allow-rule assertion incorrectly expected the overall read decision to deny in ask mode; that mode already allows reads independently of the rule, so the matcher itself is tested. Focused tests and check pass after these fixes. Existing S-1, S-2 and S-5 apply.

W385 path-observation integration (2026-10-30). The first source generator invocation omitted its required module and output directories and exited before changing output; rerunning with both arguments generated ten leaf-policy slices. The first focused test run then failed only its old terminal-loop message assertion: copied `c_` selects `Al` for an unresolved loop before `sNe`, so the result says the landing could not be determined. The assertion now checks that source-specific branch; ordinary and dangling links still require the symbolic-link message. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 approval attribution (2026-10-30). The first review note attributed a U+FFFD PermissionRequest rewrite failure to the new leaf adapter. The owned fixture confirmed the failure, but a HEAD comparison and separate direct probes showed that the configured-policy content matcher already throws before the adapter runs; the adapter introduces the equivalent early failure only when no policy calls that matcher. The record now distinguishes those two observations and preserves the static source verdict as conditional. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 identity-factory fixture (2026-10-30). The first generated `.ts` fixture was imported from a temporary directory without an ESM package boundary, so `tsx` exposed no named export and the probe did not run. Copying the identical generated bytes to a temporary `.mjs` path made the module format explicit; all four fixture cases passed. A later injected `EACCES` ancestor experiment expected the launch-ancestry projection to reveal a nested link, but both supplied ancestry states retained only the requested spelling; the corrected observation is a limited negative result, not a general equivalence claim. The source bodies and ledger are unchanged. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 approval-binding fixture (2026-10-30). The first temporary copy was imported as `.ts` outside the repository's ESM package boundary and had no named export; regenerating the unchanged source with injected error classes and using a temporary `.mjs` wrapper fixed the scratch import. The initial persistent test called the copied `readExisting` with no argument; runtime passed but TypeScript inferred one required parameter. Passing explicit `undefined` keeps the source body unchanged. The two binding groups and eight related tests pass, as does check. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 review scheduling (2026-10-30). A second independent reader was started while the source-verdict reader was already active. It was stopped immediately to restore the project's one-reader rule; no result is counted. The open-primitives review remains queued. Existing S-4 and project WORKFLOW section 6 apply.

W385 source citation (2026-10-30). A first note used estimated endpoints for `ek`, `gRt` and `Mre`; locating the following declaration and hashing the exact source corrected them to [4200,4269), [217195,217458) and [220553,220592), UTF-16. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 open-interleaving fixture (2026-10-30). The first macOS probe combined `O_NOFOLLOW` with `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` and received `EINVAL`, so it did not exercise the source flag contract. `Z3` clears the former before adding the latter. After matching that operation, the deterministic transient parent-link interleaving is refused; the ordinary-open facade returns the protected fixture before the restored spelling check. The first macOS unit test omitted the source wrapper's fifth argument: runtime passed but TypeScript rejected both calls. Supplying explicit `undefined` preserves the source body and check passes. This records one injected macOS observation and leaves capability fallback and other platforms open. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 leaf-link preparation (2026-10-30). Two searches named nonexistent file globs and stopped before searching; later lookups use existing files. An optional raw invalid-UTF-8 filename probe returned macOS `EILSEQ` and cleaned its temporary directory; it supplies no cross-platform pathname result. The first new regression fixture gave its provider a `countTokens` method and a same-named `count` field; it was corrected to the actual `ModelProvider.count` contract before running. The two baseline tests then fail on the intended outcomes: direct write permission returns allow for a leaf symlink, and a PermissionRequest rewrite reaches the real write tool without an error. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 unknown-HOME probe (2026-10-30). A first inline Node probe used JavaScript template literals around shell `${...}` syntax and failed to parse; quoted JavaScript strings retained the intended literal shell bytes on rerun. A marker passed through the source analyser remains in a simple candidate when surrounded by enough literal text, and a wildcard deny can match the marker itself. A HOME occurrence before the declaration can also stop the scan before any prefix count is recorded. An object-coercion sentinel did not identify a quoted expansion because the source returns the object without coercion, and an assignment admitted it as partial data and rendered `undefined=undefined env`. These probes disprove treating a marker alone as a complete unknown-HOME decision; no production behavior changed. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 shell startup CLI fixture (2026-10-30). The first `/bin/sh` control expanded to an empty prompt after writing an empty HOME marker, so the CLI returned `Prompt is empty`; that run was not a usable baseline. Adding ordinary authored instruction text made the prompt nonempty. The six shell/policy cases then completed and retained explicit marker, exit and model-request assertions. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 single-policy assertions (2026-10-30). The first redirection-forwarding expectation omitted the parser object's own `fd: undefined` field, which JSON display had hidden. The strict deep comparison exposed the mismatch; retaining that field makes all seven related tests pass. The added prefix-allow and independent read-only-gate cases also pass. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 approval-to-use fixture (2026-10-30). The first owned Agent probe searched a nonexistent top-level tool-result ID; the journal stores the ID as `result.tool_use_id`. After correcting the lookup, six explicit stable, retargeted and directly denied read/write cases confirm the path-approval gap. Two attempts to derive consumer extents by parsing a trailing module suffix encountered an undefined export and a truncated input; parsing the target function node directly produced the recorded ranges and body digests. A search naming a nonexistent report glob was replaced with the explicit package record. The first attempt to add this entry matched a repeated heading and changed nothing. The source-open scratch plan builder briefly retained a duplicate loop after an edit; it was removed before generation. Its five generated ranges then matched source and four owned-link assertions passed. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 custom-shell CLI fixture (2026-10-30). The first plugin probe invoked a qualified skill name while the default plugin catalog exposes the bare name. The CLI consequently sent the text to the fixture model and the expected denial assertion failed, so that run was not a product baseline. The driver now invokes the catalog's actual bare name and checks zero new model requests, an absent effect marker and a denial from the compiled CLI. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-6 apply.

W385 stash citation (2026-10-30). An initial static note attached an estimated end offset to the simulated-edit consumer. Its entry offset and observed calls are retained; the unverified range endpoint is removed. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 custom-shell HOME (2026-10-30). A real skill regression showed `/bin/zsh` supplies the account directory when HOME is absent and escaped the host's empty-HOME projection. The first two-projection fix wrapped `permissionDecision` in an outer scope, but the ordinary Shell adapter's inner scope overwrote it, so the focused test still failed. Pass the alternate directly to the sampled analyser through the outer call scope and preserve its intended owner before the nested decision. A preceding targeted search included nonexistent `src/skills.ts`; subsequent searches use existing paths. Existing S-1, S-4, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 HOME-scoped repair (2026-10-30). The first copied-analyser scope used an untyped generated callback, so TypeScript inferred unknown results in the permission and clamp callers. Move the scope to a typed host adapter and keep the recovered analyser declarations unchanged. The initial runtime assertion expected raw stdout, while the existing Shell tool appends its exit-status line; match the actual pre-mutation HOME and exclude the later value. The proposed `$PATH` bypass control remained an ask after removing the HOME guard because both source vectors report a nonliteral expansion; retain it as an explicit incomplete-vector case and use a fully analysed quoted `$HOME` control for this repair. The original red CLI and deny assertions remain. Existing S-1, S-2, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 path identity fixture (2026-10-30). The first combined scratch module placed two original `Rt` identifiers from separate source modules in one scope, so both syntax and scope scans stopped before any fixture ran. Keep the imported path predicates in their own lexical scope and expose only their original exports; bind the filesystem error predicate to its recovered `W` body. The next root-comparison fixture omitted its imported `Bn` predicate; the undeclared scan caught it before execution. A later test search named absent `test/session.test.ts` and exited 2; the session tests were located with `rg` on the existing tree. A subsequent bounded `vX` read named the wrong source module and returned no slice; the same range from the recorded `chunk-sfn1dbxq.js` supplied the body. One no-op Edit call left the record unchanged. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 path-owner search (2026-10-30). Two targeted searches included a nonexistent `src/commands.ts` argument and returned exit 2; an unrelated heading search produced excessive output. The current skill callback owners were then read directly from `src/interactive.ts`, `src/cli.ts` and `src/runtime.ts`. A first attempt to add this entry matched a repeated heading and changed nothing; this edit uses the adjacent current entry. Subsequent searches should select existing paths and bound headings or exact symbols. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 skill hook HOME (2026-10-30). An owned skill fork with two preflighted SessionStart hooks changed HOME during the second approval; the first hook then wrote the changed value despite its sampled deny rule. The new per-definition binding carries the preflight value into foreground, configured-async and adopted execution and in-memory registry resume; the parent definition stays separate. The regression first observed the wrong value and passes after the repair. Independent review ended on the agent API budget and remains open. Existing S-1, S-4, S-5 and S-6 apply.

W385 path wrapper fixture (2026-10-30). The initial scratch plan-builder declared `module` in a CommonJS file and failed before producing a plan. Rename that local variable and verify the generated plan's exact keys and source paths before reuse; a naive replacement also changed those keys and was corrected before generation. The corrected seven-range copy and the existing parser/table fixture pass six literal wrapper cases. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 absent-HOME assignment (2026-10-30). Independent review found `X=$HOME export Y` carries the Node account-directory fallback in sampled env-prefix text while `argvSourceLiteral` remains true. The first regression failed with a false deny; the actual empty assignment rule was also allowed in the reviewer probe. The adapter now holds dollar-bearing sampled commands when HOME is absent, after retaining the original reading checks. This deliberately includes literal dollars and other expansions until the source policy has a complete host environment contract. Existing S-1, S-2, S-4, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 path fixture probe (2026-10-30). An initial `tsx -e` probe used top-level await in its CJS evaluation mode and failed before running; wrapping the dynamic import in an async function allowed the owned fixture to run. The synthetic cat-only path fixture holds an outside path, but reports passthrough for `env X=1 cat ...` because its bL callback is an identity. That result is fixture scope, not source wrapper behavior. Existing S-1 and S-5 apply.

W385 skill HOME binding (2026-10-30). The new sampled-denial adapter reads HOME during preflight, but later approval callbacks could change HOME before the owned skill runner spawned an earlier command. An owned invocation reproduced a denied synthetic HOME value reaching the marker; the corresponding regression failed before the fix. Each preflight now retains its own HOME and passes it to that span's child process, including an absent value. The host's whole-body preflight remains intact. A second owned fixture reproduced the same discrepancy in foreground, background and command-monitor execution through the tool status callback. The first harness probe had an extra foreground input and used an incorrect background wait method; correcting the fixture reproduced all three paths. The authorization result now carries its HOME into each shell runner, and auto mode rechecks a changed HOME before accepting the previous classifier verdict. Existing S-1, S-2, S-4, S-5, S-6 and S-7 apply.

W385 sampled environment boundary (2026-10-29). With HOME absent, `E6` expands `$HOME` through Node's account-directory fallback, while the owned `/bin/sh` observes it as empty; the first sampled-denial candidate therefore matched a path the executed shell did not receive. A red regression reproduces the incorrect denial. A successful sampled command with dynamic argv and absent HOME now retains an uncertainty hold, and a failed sampled parse also holds an otherwise allowed command after checking existing deny readings. The prior behavior without command rules is unchanged. This is a conservative host boundary, not source equivalence. Existing S-1, S-4, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 wrapper adapter typing (2026-10-29). The source matcher accepts `ruleBehavior` at runtime, but TypeScript infers its destructured options from the copied JavaScript without that optional property. The focused tests passed while the first check rejected the direct object literal. A named options value preserves all three source arguments without changing the recovered matcher. The first declaration-sampling adapter check exposed the same inference for `E6`'s `declarationPrefix`; a named value preserves that source parameter as well. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 source citation (2026-10-29). An earlier range for the `D3e` subcommand-denial statement ended inside an expression. Re-reading the source and hashing the complete statement corrected it to UTF-16 [3262265,3262627), with the prior partial citation retained as a correction in the package record. The first question-log edit matched a historical repeated heading and changed nothing; anchoring the current entry made the edit unique. The later mixed explicit-path `git add` again reported ignored `research/` after staging the tracked paths; the two new plans were added with `git add -f` and the staged list was checked. Existing S-1, S-7 and explicit-path staging guidance apply.

W385 rule-store plan (2026-10-29). A manually transcribed `Bc` digest omitted two hexadecimal characters; the source generator rejected the mismatch before writing any output. Replacing it with the measured source digest allowed all six bound ranges to generate. The initial focused tests passed while TypeScript inferred the copied matcher results as `unknown[]` and rejected a narrower test helper; explicit runtime shape assertions narrow those values without changing the copied source. A later manually transcribed `Vcn` digest also mismatched; the generator again stopped before writing and the measured digest corrected it. A first `GTr` probe passed `{text}` objects where the copied matcher expects analysed commands with `argv` and `envVars`, and it threw before completing. The owned `w6`/`E6` analyser supplies the full shape for the corrected probe. The first early-rule tests passed at runtime but TypeScript flagged nullable results and the broad inferred decision union; explicit assertions narrow the actual result and rule shape. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply; future plans should derive digests mechanically from the verified ranges.

W385 scheduler typing (2026-10-29). The copied `ior` signature has four positional parameters and TypeScript therefore required the fourth argument in the test, even though JavaScript permits it to be omitted. The initial runtime tests passed but `npm run check` failed; passing explicit `undefined` fixed the test without changing source statements. Check and the 14 affected tests pass. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 staging (2026-10-29). A mixed `git add` naming tracked research files reported the ignored `research/` directory even though the tracked paths and other named files were staged. The two new source plans were then staged with explicit `git add -f`; the staged path list was inspected before committing. Existing explicit-path staging guidance applies.

W385 policy review (2026-10-29). An independent reader identified a multi-directory early ask before the `jcn` deny check and proposed a conditional final ask. Main then traced the surrounding `$cn` ask recheck and ran the copied `Kg` splitter; it emits the denied `rm target` text, so the source recheck denies under the stated exact-rule conditions. Early function returns must be followed through wrappers before declaring a final policy result. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply.

W385 source-port typing (2026-10-29). The exact JavaScript declaration of `E6` makes TypeScript infer an option shape containing only `plainUnquotedHeredocs`, so the first test run passed while `npm run check` rejected direct `declarationPrefix` literals. A test helper supplies both fields without changing the copied source; check and the two analyser tests then passed. Source range identity remains unchanged. Existing S-1 and S-7 apply.

W385 source-port fixture (2026-10-29). A Unicode string before a prefixed declaration produced `too-complex` for the false vector while the true vector produced `echo new`. The first fixture expected both vectors to be simple and failed. Direct source-module observations identified the different outcomes; the probe now asserts each result and passes. This preserves the source outcome without inferring why the parser and analyser differ. Existing S-1, S-5 and S-7 apply. The module remains unconnected to permissions. See [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md).

W385 (2026-10-29). The W373 proposed predicate marked every declaration prefix incomplete, but the source enumerates up to three positions exhaustively. The compiled CLI runs the three- and four-prefix full-line allows and denies a separately denied fourth command. A copied-source fixture additionally shows a prefix vector changes a later command's text. An initial hand-written helper changed a Unicode escape's spelling; exact source-byte verification caught it and a generated copy replaced it. An independent reader found the first fixture checked hashes only after imports and had no expected-value assertions; a new runner checks five file hashes before import and asserts the recorded vectors, texts and semantics. The reader did not inspect the later-value output or source ledgers; main checked those separately. Product and build identity remain unchanged. Existing S-1/S-5/S-7 apply; no product repair is claimed. See [W385](ports/W385-declaration-prefix-preflight.md).

W383 (2026-10-29). The compiled MCP persistence driver expected bare `/fork` to switch the active session and used the last `session:` as the fork ID. The actual background fork prints a full log path to stdout, keeps the parent and writes a branch journal; the baseline failed with the parent's ID. A temporary owned probe confirms the fork path and branch owner, and explicit resume reaches the original SIGKILL acceptance chain. The first edited driver then intermittently failed a no-new-announcement assertion: `ready` waited for the banner, while the interactive loop's second startup announcement occurs afterward. Its exact late stderr contents were not retained, so the cause remains an inference. `ready` now waits for the interactive announcement before sending commands. The first failure is preserved in W383; no product behavior changed. An independent static reviewer found the negative stderr check could miss a late event and the fork-history assertion was absent. The driver uses `/status` for parent ownership, compares the copied conversation events and checks the resumed request. Follow-up static review identified duplicate user messages and a request-count gap before poll; the driver now checks all ten user strings in order and counts the entire resume-to-poll interval. The compiled SIGKILL chain passes. Existing S-1/S-5/S-6 apply.

W376 (2026-10-28). The old Hook PTY driver timed out waiting for full status strings that the current TUI clips and replaces; the first journal gate also searched stderr even though the PTY merges it into stdout. The driver now uses journal records for completion and a 160-column PTY to check the visible TeammateIdle system message and hidden output. A follow-up checks only newline-complete records and parses each complete record strictly, matching S-5 rather than swallowing malformed lines. Full-line context and PostCompact assertions were replaced by explicit journal assertions and a new status-sink test; the record states this coverage boundary. PostCompact source `avt` [2932807,2933599) renders the raw record, so a structured `suppressOutput` is not treated as an applied field. The first full suite failed MCP stream test `test/mcp-notification-stream.test.ts:313` (1 instead of 2 responses); an isolated 6/6 and an unchanged full rerun 3458/3458 passed. The cause of the intermittent failure remains unproven. The initial W376 write-up overclaimed complete Hooks-family acceptance and omitted a Notification hook-only call site; both claims are narrowed. The independent reader disconnected before returning a verdict; its retained analysis identified three real verification defects: manual compaction's own `turn_end` could satisfy the after-turn gate, other gates accepted any end reason, and a PostCompact output assertion matched the payload in the command label. The compact debug journal confirms indices compact 4, manual_compact 7, after-prompt 8, end_turn 10. The driver now binds prompt, session and `end_turn`, waits for manual compaction before sending the next prompt, polls while the PTY is quiet, and checks the post-compact journal. The test reads raw output from a short-named fixture file, leaving its payload absent from the label. Its first longer marker was clipped under the existing 96-byte budget; a short `RAW` marker retains the exact output-prefix and truncation checks. All compiled modes and the 61-test lifecycle suite pass after repair. The independent `VERIFIED_SCOPED` review noted that the background skill's parent command was not bound to its result. Reading `interactive.ts` and the registry established the local command's terminal descriptor as the actual completion result, with no parent model `turn_end`. A `TerminalScreen` decoder checks its ID against the completed child journal; the compiled children route passes at `cbe7f1ad`. The final bounded independent review verified that ID binding but identified a real observer race: before a new journal's first newline is written, `slice(0, -1)` parses truncated metadata and may crash the driver. A negative boundary now yields no records until a complete line is present; a focused probe checks empty, partial, complete-plus-partial, and malformed complete input. A follow-up inventory mistakenly parsed the Markdown partial-closure matrix as JSON; selecting its actual table row corrected that observer. A first count then scanned all tables in FEATURES and counted two historical status rows; restricting it to the current Family/Status table confirms 37 families, 15 Integrated, 18 Partial, two External dependency and two Excluded. Neither observation changed the product or evidence. Exact-path `git add` returned an ignored-research warning even though it staged all seven tracked paths; inspecting the index confirmed them, then the two new files were staged separately with `-f` only for the ignored port record. A later read of plugin-chain-types used an offset beyond EOF; the symbol's actual location was selected with `rg` and the bounded range was read. Re-staging the same tracked research paths emitted the same ignored-directory warning; `git diff` confirmed the requested updates were staged. Existing S-1/S-2/S-3/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply. `apply_patch` is absent in this environment; dedicated Edit/Write is the existing fallback.

W382 (2026-10-28). Compiled owned fixtures confirm two previously unverified concurrent adoption failures: identical held imports publish two records and empty reconciliation leaves one orphan; removing the declaration before a held import finishes still allows the old record to become active. A third compiled fixture shows a replacement loads first, then the old load overwrites its owner, so removal leaves the replacement orphaned. Fixtures retire any retained record and delete their temporary directories. Recovered `de`/`NDe` uses a failure-neutral internal tail, and the source load/prune/reload/replace operations share that queue; the independent reader confirmed exact owners and lifecycle. The host's synchronous notification reentry and exception contracts do not directly match scheduling every operation behind a Promise. A per-name loading cache does not by itself resolve removal or replacement. The selected adapter queues each captured declaration set in source order, returns each caller's failure independently, and holds the next operation for all siblings. Existing synchronous throw assertions become Promise-rejection assertions; listener dispatch remains synchronous while its nested request queues. The first new red fixture left an orphan between subcases and contaminated later counts; its teardown now retires only records under its own fixture directory, producing separate duplicate 2/1, removal 1/0 and replacement 2/1 failures. The first independent review additionally found that a removal notification throw leaves later owners live, and duplicate names within one declaration array leave an orphan. Both reproduce in one isolated fixture before repair. Source `iF` removes the entire inventory set and retires it before notification; the host now batches removal notification after retirement. Its already-read installed-index validation rejects duplicate names, so the adoption boundary applies that validation before mutations; the source `a_e` itself is not claimed to reject duplicates. Repaired related 58/58, check/build, compiled seven-scenario probe, CLI toggle 42/42 and full 3475/3475 pass. Fresh independent review verified those repairs but identified that deleting all owners before a retirement throw strands a later live record. An injected regression reproduces it; its first teardown also contaminated the next subcase, so the fixture now explicitly restores the injected method and retires only its own orphan. Source `iF`/`tGe` was reread; the narrow adapter now retains later owners on the exceptional path, while the first throwing environment remains a separate unresolved boundary. Related 59/59, check/build, eight compiled cases, CLI 42/42 and full 3476/3476 pass. Final independent review verifies later-owner retention and points out that the injected first record's retirement function was restored but never called; fixture cleanup now calls it and focused 9/9 plus check pass. Product/build remain unchanged. A prior successful removal's notification can also be skipped by a later retirement throw, consistent with source `iF` ordering; that boundary remains open. Existing S-1/S-4/S-7 apply.

W381 (2026-10-28). A compiled notification-listener failure leaves a file module in the live inventory before its unload handle reaches the registry. A same-declaration retry produces two records; removing declarations removes only the second, leaving the first environment. The owned probe removes and retires its orphan afterward. Source `eVe`/`Pe`/`iF` and the `gs` subscriber body show that publication precedes notification and that the source inventory owns the published record; no rollback contract is established. Source and an independent ownership review preceded product edits. The host hands the unload handle to its registry owner before notification, and drops the owner before unloading. Compiled red/green, the listener failure regression, related 48/48, check/build and actual CLI toggle 42/42 pass at the first candidate. Independent review found that a throwing earlier listener prevents the mounted editor from being notified and same-declaration retry skips it; the compiled probe confirms false/false/true for after-failure, after-retry and after-manual-notification. Source `a_e` sets `isSetRecord` only after `Xye`; its unchanged fast path requires that flag, so the interrupted notification needs a host pending-state adapter. A compiled follow-up found that a synchronous same-declaration reentry could complete an inner notification before the outer notification throws, wrongly marking the entry complete; the repair adds an in-flight state and retries only after the outer failure. An overlapping-adoption probe independently reproduced the pre-existing duplicate-name candidate but failed to observe one returned Promise and exited on its unhandled rejection; its fixture was removed and no completion claim is made for that probe. The subsequent independent repair review found a different batch boundary: a sibling load starts before a later pending notification throws synchronously, bypassing registration of the all-settled barrier. A compiled main probe confirms the synchronous error followed by an unhandled missing-module rejection; the reviewer also observed a valid sibling completing after settlement had returned. The source `a_e` awaits loads under its guarded operation; the concurrent host adapter now registers every started load's barrier in `finally` even if a later synchronous notification fails. Compiled red/green missing-module and held healthy-sibling probes, related 49/49, check/build and actual CLI toggle 42/42 pass. A final independent review found removal notification can fail before the mounted editor hears it, and retrying the empty declaration has no per-module owner left to carry pending state. The compiled red confirms the stale editor after removal and retry. Since insertion and removal share a missed inventory notification, the adapter now uses inventory-scoped state and leaves nested mutations pending. Compiled removal, nested and ordinary-repeat probes, related 49/49, check/build and actual CLI toggle 42/42 pass. A fresh independent review then found a later listener can synchronously remove modules after the editor saw insertion, leaving it stale until another explicit reconciliation. The main compiled red reproduces this listener ordering; the adapter now drains nested changes after successful notification as source `gs` publishes changed snapshots synchronously. A compiled green, related 50/50, check/build and actual CLI toggle 42/42 pass. Fresh independent repair review returns VERIFIED_SCOPED after three regressions and compiled failure/reentry/settlement fixtures; it did not rerun the complete related suites or CLI. The source reader used 12 calls against an eight-call target; the first implementation reviewer used 43 against 12 and the final review 48 against 12. Existing S-1/S-4/S-7 apply.

W380 (2026-10-28). An installed local hooks module that throws causes the compiled CLI to exit 1 before `/status`; `loadProjectConfig` discards the asynchronous `applyPluginHooks` result. Recovered `a_e` catches per-module failures and continues, while direct `vVe` reload propagates its failure. Source dependency, error presentation and the healthy-sibling startup contract are under investigation before product changes. A broad search across `config.test.ts` exceeded the bounded output target; subsequent reads use the exact fixture and owner. The first regression fixture re-imported a failed cached module before the delayed failing sibling settled, then an inert fixture omitted its required hooks JSON; waiting for settlement and writing the required file reached the intended unhandled config rejection. The first type check found the test's filtered union still typed `message` as unknown; narrowing inside `flatMap` preserved the assertion. The sibling CLI green probe initially waited on an open stdin after startup succeeded; sending `/status` and `/exit` confirmed exit 0 and the healthy module. Independent static review found that the newly added error record invokes synchronous host listeners, so a throwing listener replaces the module error for an explicit caller. A compiled red probe confirmed the diagnostic remains in the sink but the caller receives the listener error. Recovered `vVe` rethrows the original module error; a `finally` in the host reporting adapter now preserves it, with a new regression, compiled green probe, check/build and both CLI startup probes passing. The first repair reviewer exceeded its time budget and was stopped with no verdict. A second reviewer returned INCONCLUSIVE after checking `finally` and settlement, without checking the host write order or source identity; an exact-gap follow-up verified the host write order, recovered source and identity, with no test or CLI rerun. The lazy-registry-import failure remains a separately recorded diagnostic gap. Follow-up source inspection shows `r8` reaches the `zz` log/UI sink and gated `o3` notice; `fc` reads a separate catalog `plugins.errors` array. The mapping from a runtime module failure to a panel row is unproven and must be traced before connecting those states. Existing S-1/S-4/S-7 apply.

W379 (2026-10-28). A relay mounted before real file-module adoption remained unhooked despite `chainHooked` returning true. Compiled and focused source regressions reproduced it. The recovered `Pe`/`vVe`/`iF` → `Xye` → `rIn` → `r5t` → `wc` store notification identifies the missing update; file load and actual removal now publish through the existing host listener and advance the render version. Related 71/71 plus classic consumers 13/13, check/build, compiled toggle 42/42 and live PTY install 5/5 pass at identity `664fc876`. Independent review VERIFIED_SCOPED reports a retirement-throw boundary; source `iF` also calls retire before notification. Its follow-up identified a load-notification throw after inventory insertion. A compiled probe first failed because it had not initialized the registry's environment factory; after importing the real registry, a throwing listener reproduced one orphaned record and one environment, which the probe then cleaned. Existing S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 apply.

W378 (2026-10-28). A failed module load left the process-wide settlement promise rejected after the caller had received the failure and removed the declaration; the initial regression also reported an unhandled rejection from that retained promise. A separate all-settled barrier tracks every started load while preserving the caller's rejection. The first related run then failed `relay.isRecording()`: the fixture mounted the relay before file adoption, but the actual file loader mutates the inventory without the listener notification used by `installChainModule`. Mounting after the surviving module loads isolates the settlement predicate; the missing live file-load notification is a separate confirmed gap. A compiled probe shows `chainHooked: true` and `relayRecording: false` after loading a file into a mounted relay; recovered `Pe`/`vVe`/`iF` update `loadedModules` then call the `Xye` → `rIn` → `r5t` → `wc` version-store notification chain, with exact coordinates and digests in W378. A broad search over the minified source emitted an oversized line; exact UTF-16 slicing retrieved the target. Independent review returned VERIFIED_SCOPED and reran both new tests 2/2. It retained the uncaught config startup Promise and missing sibling diagnostics as separate gaps and an untested same-name concurrent adoption as a candidate. Existing S-1/S-4/S-5/S-7 apply.

## W285 independent review repairs

An inherited low session reloaded a new xhigh model default into `modelSettings`, but left the
derived `config.effort` at low. The feedback resolver displayed xhigh while Agent.request sent low.
The review probe and an existing reload regression extension reproduce the mismatch before repair.
The source `uc` re-reads its settings table for inherit, so reloadConfig now refreshes only inherited
snapshots, including the legacy fallback, after replacing the table. Manual/default remain pinned.

At 80 columns, the new feedback column truncated a pre-padded full-width status string and removed
the running interrupt hint. A decoded before/after probe reproduced that loss. The source footer
layout allocates columns before laying out text. The footer adapter now requests status text with
the remaining width before the existing padding rule runs. S-4/S-6 source owner/reader repairs.

## W285 feedback placement and patch correction

The provisional footer adapter passed the deadline probe but hid a later `status fixture` message
while effort feedback remained active. Decoded terminal rows preserve that result. The owning source
consumer is being traced before changing placement; `p$t` only supplies a boolean, and `lde` renders
the goal indicator rather than a notification. The source-read result must decide the display seam.
The initial combined patch failed atomically on a footer hunk and was reapplied with exact positions.
`refreshEffortUi` was briefly nested inside `bindConfigWatch`; direct scope readback moved it outside
before the first successful typecheck. These are S-1 placement diagnosis and S-6 caller-scope repairs.
The standalone formatter and lifecycle probe pass, including expiry at 10000 ms, same-label dedup,
replacement deadline cancellation and brief-mode removal/restoration.

The first compiled driver passed expiry, model, slider, ultracode, focus and request checks, then
waited for the full reload status text. The decoded row ends at `Local configuration reloa` because
the permission chip and hints reserve width. The driver now waits on that observed prefix and
rebinds saved focus/default modes to give the gate check a distinguishing counterexample. Reloading
an unchanged expired label must stay expired; a blanket expectation of a visible label was wrong.
The fixture was corrected, preserving the product's dependency-based dedup contract. S-1/S-5.

The next compiled run reached the environment override checks, then startup rejected `LLM_EFFORT=unset`
with the existing strict effort parser before the TUI mounted. This package does not change that

wire effort without an environment override. The `effortOverride` unit still covers its runtime
`unset` value; no compiled claim is made for that pre-mount parser path. S-1 fixture boundary.

The themed-cell fixture initially assigned `ge.level` directly, although W284 already established

## W284 fixture and recovered color typing corrections

The first history-tag tests expected 83 trailing rule cells after a 16-cell prefix on a 100-column
row. The decoded frame measured 84. The assertion now pads the measured prefix to the actual width.
A second test called an absent `restorePrompt` method; it now sends Ctrl-U and actual replacement
text. TypeScript also exposes the copied color proxy as a bare callable, so the dim builder is read
with `Reflect.get(ge, 'dim')`, preserving its receiver and the recovered rendering call.
The dim-cell assertion then exposed color level zero in the non-TTY test process. The fixture now
sets level 3 and restores the prior value in cleanup, as the existing palette tests do.
These were fixture/API and dependency typing failures. No source behavior or existing expectation changed.

## W235 resolved ownership and display adaptation

The baseline's two defects are repaired and bound to the compiled run. Config Verbose persists and
applies through a successor session; applyConfig refreshes the live terminal from the accepted
session, so toggling repaints existing rows. `/focus` now writes viewMode then applies a successor
session, and the previously failing next turn completes two provider calls with no runtime-owner
error. Startup precedence is stored-first: `It` reads `o.verbose ?? viewMode fallback`, so the
earlier baseline claim that viewMode governs a new process was wrong and the record is corrected.
Tool display is live-only: the host does not replay historical messages on resume, so tool rows are
not recreated and the record states that boundary. Read's F is summary-only even in verbose mode;
the detailed lane is list/search (qa) and failure (Jf). The recovered qa hint is `(ctrl+o)` with no
ellipsis, and its verbose header and `$e` gutter carry NBSPs; both were corrected after reading
qa, wu/wy, xz and $e again. A blank five-cell spacer sometimes remains after a detailed tool entry
in the host screen; it carries no content.

The full suite's one failure, `CLI effort flag overrides environment/settings and resume retains the
pinned level`, fails identically at HEAD5717eb9 in a detached worktree. W231's wire-level
`effortOverride` gives `LLM_EFFORT` precedence over the CLI flag. It is independent of W235 and was
not repaired here.

## W235 compiled display and mode ownership baseline

The owned compiled read_file probe at HEAD5717eb9 delivered three marker lines to the provider
but rendered no tool output with saved viewMode=verbose. After a completed tool turn, /focus on
wrote viewMode in place; the next task made no provider call and reported Runtime owner
configuration changed. The source focus command emits apply_flag_settings after the write, so
W235 will use the existing successor-session application path. The first probe waited for the
full error string at 90 columns and timed out because the footer visibly ends at Runtime own;
the captured frame and unchanged request count retain the deciding evidence. The final driver
will bind the error outcome to request/journal state and the visible prefix. Causes S-4 owner
boundary and S-5 measured footer width. The source reader initially closed Config-to-thinking;
its full-tool frontier is delegated as a separate bounded read before any tool-display edit.

## W234 fixture and paging corrections

The initial focused run found three fixture mismatches. Media inputs require a workspace-relative
path, so the attachment fixture now passes pixel.png. Its provider did not honor the signal and
made the aborted manual call appear successful; the provider now checks the real caller signal at
count/complete. Typechecking also required the current SDK message metadata and usage fields,
which were added directly from the installed declaration without a cast. Adding the first Config
row changed the measured page extent. A direct xterm frame shows one PageDown reaches the question
timeout and a second reaches Dialog expiry; the regression now checks both pages and returns with
two PageUp events, preserving its original last-row coverage. Causes: S-1 fixture admission,
S-4 signal ownership and S-6 driver/schema migration. apply_patch is absent, so dedicated Edit/Write
remain the exact-edit fallback. A repeated Config fallback string required replace_all after the
unique replacement guard caught three matches. The first compiled chain passed ten toggle/request/
manual/restart checks, then supplied --user-dir together with --resume. cli.ts explicitly locks
that launch flag on resume. The driver now supplies user-dir and gateway setup only for new
sessions and retains the stored configuration on resume, preserving the snapshot contract. No product behavior was
changed to fit a fixture. The last default-concurrency run canceled the existing effort test at
its 8-second timeout while other tests were active. Its exact isolated rerun passed, and a final
file-concurrency=2 run passes all 164 tests; no timeout or product change was made for it.
The second compiled pass reached sixteen checks, then waited for full
capacity-error text that is clipped in the 90-column footer. The decoded frame already showed
ready plus error: Input excee. The driver now waits on that measured prefix and checks zero
completion requests; full error text remains covered at the Agent boundary. This is S-5 display
assertion targeting. A further compiled use-chain check changed /autocompact to 200k while auto
was disabled. The save succeeded, but the next turn produced Runtime owner configuration changed
and zero completions. The existing threshold handler mutated session.config.compactAt in place;
runtime.ts ownerConfigDigest/buildTools correctly rejects that changed fingerprint. Recovered
$2t, agx4redd [1968,3167), persists first then emits apply_flag_settings to refresh its owner.
Adapt that event through the product's existing successor-session applyConfig, preserving the
stored boolean and history. This is a confirmed S-4/S-6 live configuration ownership defect,
covered by the retained compiled threshold-change/next-task probe. Independent review then found
two capacity-path gaps: the newly blocked turn remained awaitingResponse, preventing manual
compact, and a custom compactAt above capacity let an oversized summary pass the old summary
check. Both were reproduced on the compiled Agent at d4e5b3b8. L$e keeps blocked independent, so
the summary check now requires both capacity and compactAt. Recovered yd [147582,148075) returns
blocking_limit with Prompt is too long, and QH [125441,126326) records that terminal result. The
host now uses Agent.finish rather than throwing, freeing awaitingResponse while preserving the
blocked input for manual summary. The regression and compiled chain also compact and continue
after this result. Causes: S-4 terminal turn ownership and S-6 every capacity consumer. After threshold ownership changed, one older interactive test
still inspected the closed original session and expected its compactAt to mutate. It now reads
the sole active lock's metadata and keeps an explicit assertion that the original snapshot stays
512000. This preserves its live-window and cap/reset assertions under the corrected owner model.
An intermediate affected run passes 164/164. The original independent summary probe then
confirmed the new capacity guard still threw after the human input was appended. Changed the
shared compact result to the typed compacted/blocking_limit outcome and adapted both automatic
callers to finish the blocked turn, following the same acquired yd failed-compaction branch.
Manual compact surfaces its existing failure while retaining a closed turn. The same three
regression groups and compiled driver now cover manual repair and next input after an oversized
summary. The reviewer session ended with an upstream 502 after saving its original inputs and
seven-group CLI result; those files remain usable for independent repair recheck. A final fresh
input probe at capacity980616/count980700/compactAt981000 found the old short-history throw was
also reached by the newly added hard boundary. That exact hard-capacity case now returns the
same source blocking_limit terminal result; the lower automatic-threshold guard stays unchanged.
The existing regression group and CLI driver now assert zero summary calls and a closed turn for
this initial-input case. This is the same S-4/S-6 outcome propagation gap, not a new algorithm.
Two malformed Edit argument calls and a nonunique helper string
were rejected before mutation; the final edit used a unique local anchor.

## W233 config panel integration and source corrections

Initial typechecking found a missing te import in the new frame and an optional adjusted value
passed to isEffortLevel. Added the declared symbol and narrowed the answer before validation.
The first live TUI probe reached the last narrow row and preserved the draft, but batched
`/time` entered search and discarded the remaining characters. Gl receives individual key events;
the host now processes subsequent characters under the newly active search owner. The original
probe hung waiting for the unopened subpage and was stopped with its tracked task handle.
Direct mn/fn/Qe comparison also found the preliminary page/wheel code only approximated the
source viewport. Ported the ungrouped boundary/window algorithm and its wheel focus clamp.
wn gives picker links accept/next without previous; left now remains idle. Unregistered Settings
retry/stats bindings no longer swallow printable search-entry characters. Causes: S-6 decoded
input ownership and S-7 source branch completeness. apply_patch remains absent; dedicated
Edit/Write tools preserve exact edits and unrelated changes.
The first compiled acceptance reached 24 passing checks, then its 20-column open predicate waited
for an unwrapped Search settings string. The decoded frame already showed the panel and wrapped
placeholder. Switched that predicate to the source prefix plus search guide, then ported Vc's
source display clipping and literal prefix before the final run. No persistence assertion was
removed. The effort map exposed persistableEffort's too-wide return type; narrowed it to exclude
max instead of casting the value. The source comparison tool initially matched a local variable
named key as well as the method; restricted variable matches to arrow-function declarations.
The corrected compiled run passes 29 checks, and all twelve source comparisons bind exact AST
ranges. Causes: S-5 display invariant and S-6 authoritative type/AST consumer migration.
Independent review then confirmed six source/consumer gaps. Added per-model default loading made
the old standalone /effort top-level writer ineffective after restart; save/auto now also update
or delete the active map leaf when that map exists. uBe ys/uc requires inherited effort to follow
the focused model until manual adjustment; explicit inherit/level/default session state now
preserves that distinction in the picker and model application. The settings parser's passthrough
also carried unconsumed authored model fields into journals; the parser now projects only effort,
while the disk writer retains unknown fields. ke ownership must match the loader's descriptor
admission, so symlinked higher layers now hide the timeout rows. Time format retains its enum
options for St filtering even though it opens a picker. An accepted write error after immediate
Ctrl+C now produces a transcript notice before the modal's callback releases. Causes: S-6 all
consumer synchronization, S-4 async outcome ownership, S-7 source state/row shape completeness.
The first close recheck searched raw ANSI for a contiguous JSON error and failed even though the
decoded frame displayed the whole notice. Bound that assertion to the decoded frame. A journal
check similarly matched unrelated text in tool schemas; it now checks metadata.config.modelSettings
as a structured projection. Both are S-5 assertion-target corrections. The expanded compiled
Config driver passes 35 checks, including all six review reproductions, and the repaired related
suite passes 264/264. Final review and identity are recorded in the package record.

## W232 model picker source and consumer corrections

The preliminary search candidate read startInSearchMode alone. Direct closure of Gse and its uBe
caller proved canEnter=false gates both initialization and entry. Withdrew search before product
edits and kept the reachable list, focus, manual effort and acceptance paths. Cause: S-7, caller
gate must be read with the dependency body. The first W232 check found the old regression still
importing modelPickerEffortFor after its value-equality API was deleted. Migrated that consumer to
actual owner actions, keeping untouched omission and adding round-trip/endpoint touched cases.
Cause: S-6, migrate tests with the changed state API. Continued source comparison found lc's
previous-at-window-top and page positioning differed from generic focus-fit; adapted those exact
branches before acceptance. apply_patch remains absent, so dedicated Edit/Write preserve the
existing unrelated working tree changes.
Independent review reproduced hidden model rows at 20x24 and 24x24: the wrapped caption consumed
the fixed body budget even though the owner advertised one visible option. Source uBe budgets
at least two options; the extra fixed transcript/crop is host-only. Reproduced the exact frames,
then gave the modal its measured caption/effort/minimum-list space before assigning transcript
rows and measured wrapped option heights before choosing the visible count. Added narrow resize,
last-row and pointer coverage. Cause: S-3/S-4, host frame budget discarded the focused source row.
The first CLI cleanup assertion looked in .harness/sessions rather than Session.open's .agent.
Checked the actual seven journals and zero .jsonl.lock files, then corrected the driver to require
existing journals before asserting lock release. Cause: S-1/S-5, cleanup probe used an assumed path.
The compiled narrow run first waited for ready text clipped by the permission chip; the captured
20-column composer proved idle. The driver now waits for that actual narrow composer/permission
prefix. A later narrow click selected model-01 at index 12: the prior current-model promotion and
recent-session roster had reordered the list, so the positional fixture expectation was false.
Read the interactive roster producer, reset the narrow phase to model-01, and keep both the painted
number and request identity assertions. Causes: S-5/S-6, viewport and state-dependent test inputs.

## W231 slider verification

The first compiled slider run expected the max note to contain `Use sparingly for the hardest tasks.`
on one physical row. At 90 columns the source renderer wraps after `sparingly`; the captured frame
contains the complete text. The assertion now folds whitespace only for that prose comparison.
Track width, triangle coordinates, persistence bytes and request fields remain exact assertions.
The next run treated the ordinary 90-cell composer separator as a slider because its regexp only
required 42 track characters. The exact slider predicate now requires one triangle and exactly 42
trimmed cells; the captured cancellation frame already showed ready and the restored composer.
Cause: S-5, viewport-dependent/ambiguous display assertions. No product edit was needed for these
fixture repairs.
The full override lane then reproduced a product defect: after LLM_EFFORT=high and selecting low,
the report claimed high remained effective but the request carried low. Source cln -> kE -> w0e
resolves the environment at request construction; Agent.request serialized chosen state directly.
Added that resolution at the request boundary without erasing the session choice, with separate
concrete/null/missing cases. The same source read distinguished invalid/empty Y0 inputs from the
explicit auto/unset sentinel, so the local parser now preserves that distinction too. New public
Agent run/count regression and the compiled override request both cover the repaired consumer.
Cause: S-6/S-7, missing effective-value consumer after command state application.
The added Agent reply fixture first omitted SDK-required nullable fields and failed tsc although
runtime tests passed. Read the installed Message/TextBlock/Usage declarations and filled those fields
without adding an assertion cast. Cause: S-6, typed fixture producer omitted required schema members.

## W230 editor integration and fixture updates

The first typecheck found literal Unicode line/paragraph separators inside a copied character regexp.
JSON transport decoded the escape spelling before writing the TypeScript source. Replaced those
patterns with explicit escaped RegExp constructor strings; the source character sets now compile.
The first panel test run still expected W229's absent add row, Back-only cards and enter-to-view notes.
Updated those assertions to the W230 source states while retaining row order, count, source and cancel
coverage; three new groups exercise save/cancel/validation/conflicts and disk reload. Before the CLI
run, traced openFile's running gate and moved the environment handoff out of withTurn, matching the
existing /keybindings caller. The 16-check compiled CLI chain and 156 affected regressions pass.
Independent review then found two host gaps. A failed optimistic edit returned a message but retained
its old layer snapshot; the next panel reopened from that same config. Refresh the user layer from
disk on open and after both write outcomes, matching di's ve/me refresh. Also, applyConfig creates a
new Session and cleared the recent-denial provider. Carry its records in newest-first order through
that branch, matching pEe's application lifetime. The compiled acceptance now includes external
edit/conflict/reopen and a live classifier denial surviving a successful rule save.
The expanded builtin-view CLI check first waited for its report text below the long card's viewport.
The recorded screen proved the card had returned. Replaced that viewport-dependent wait with the
editor's recorded export path/content, returned card focus and unchanged settings bytes. Environment
working-file cleanup remains separate from deliberately retained builtin exports.
S-1/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7 cover these causes. apply_patch is absent; dedicated Edit/Write preserve unrelated edits.

## W182 normal wrapper exit descendant cleanup

The independent owned compiled probe reproduced `fresh external` plus resumed TTY with a live orphan descendant. Abort-only PPID enumeration missed normal exit and lost ancestry after reparenting. Verified recovered Zie/$j ranges and Q-053 before adapting the Node host: random inherited per-launch marker, marker/start-identity recheck before individual signals, shared normal-close/abort settlement before private file removal and TTY restoration. Extended the existing cancellation group; 62/62 affected, check/build and compiled question/root/cancellation PTY passed; original probe now reports descendantAlive false. Independent coordinator recheck remains pending. Marker-stripping children, Windows normal-exit orphans and host enumeration failure recovery remain explicit limitations. Raw logs and final identity are recorded in ports/W182-question-editor-evidence.json.
